DONATION AGREEMENT 
 
This Donation Agreement ("Agreement") is made and entered into this _____ day of 
____________, 2022 ("Execution Date"), by and between UNION PACIFIC RAILROAD 
COMPANY, a Delaware corporation, whose address is 1400 Douglas Street, Omaha, Nebraska 
68179 ("Donor"), and the City of Cheyenne, a Wyoming municipal corporation, whose address 
is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001(" Donee"). 
 
RECITALS: 
 
Donor owns the equipment listed on Exhibit A attached hereto and made a part hereof (the 
“Equipment”). 
 
Donor desires to donate the Equipment to Donee, and Donee desires to receive and accept 
the Equipment from Donor. 
 
AGREEMENT: 
 
In exchange for the mutual covenants and commitments contained herein, the 
Donor and Donee agree as follows: 
 
Section 1.  Donation of the Equipment. 
 
Donor hereby agrees to donate to Donee, and Donee hereby agrees to receive and accept 
from Donor, subject to and on the following terms and conditions, the Equipment. 
 
Section 2.  Conditions Precedent to Donation. 
 
This Agreement is made and executed by the parties hereto subject to the following 
conditions precedent: 
 
(a) 
Asbestos Studies.  Donor has taken steps to have all of the units included in the 
Equipment inspected or evaluated for the presence of lead paint, asbestos or asbestos-containing 
materials. Donor is in possession of reports of the results of such inspection or evaluation, and 
Donor has provided a copy of the reports to Donee.   
 
(b)  Donor to Prepare Equipment for Delivery.  Donee acknowledges that Donor must 
complete certain repair and maintenance activities with respect to many of the units included in 
the Equipment in order for the Equipment to be in a condition suitable for safe delivery by rail to 
Donee. Once Donor identifies any such repair and maintenance activities, if Donor is not willing, 
in Donor’s sole discretion, to incur the cost of such repair and maintenance activities for any unit 
included in the Equipment, then Donor shall notify Donee of the cost of such activities for any 
such units and any reimbursement of such costs Donor may require as a condition of this donation.  
If Donee notifies Donor of Donee’s willingness to reimburse such costs, then the parties shall 
proceed with Closing of each such unit as well as the other units included in the Equipment. 
Provided, however, if Donee is not willing to reimburse such costs, then either Donor or Donee 
3:36 pm, Feb 13 2023

may terminate this Agreement with respect to the particular unit or units for which neither Donor 
nor Donee is willing to incur the costs of repair and maintenance, in which case neither party shall 
have any obligations under this Agreement with respect to any units for which this Agreement has 
been terminated (and the parties shall proceed to Closing on the remaining units in the Equipment).  
If Donee will be reimbursing any costs of Donor to repair and maintain any units included in the 
Equipment, then Donee will have the right to audit Donor’s costs for such work.  
 
(c)  
Donee Access to Donor Property. If Donee, with its agents or contractors, desires 
to enter upon Donor’s property in order to review or inspect the units included in the Equipment 
prior to Closing, such entry and review and inspection shall be at Donor’s sole discretion.  If Donor 
approves such entry for such review and inspection, then Donee and its agents or contractors must 
first comply with Donor’s customary right of entry terms and conditions, as set forth in Donor’s 
Contractor’s Right of Entry. 
  
Section 3. Closing; Possession. 
 
(a) 
The donation of the Equipment shall close ("Close" or "Closing") on a date or dates 
agreed upon by Donor and Donee promptly after Donor notifies Donee that the conditions set forth 
in subsection 2(b) and, if applicable, subsection 2(c) are satisfied with respect to all of the 
Equipment, and the Donor is able to deliver such units to Donee.  Donor and Donee acknowledge 
a shared intention to complete the donation and delivery of the Equipment not later than February 
28, 2023.  However, Donor and Donee acknowledge the timing of completion of repairs of the 
units for delivery to Donee, in addition to other priorities of Donor, may result in a delay in the 
Closing. 
 
(b) 
Immediately upon Closing, Donor shall deliver the Equipment to Donee by moving 
such Equipment to the location identified by the Donee.  Donee shall have no obligation to 
reimburse the transportation costs incurred by Donor in moving the Equipment.  
 
(c) 
Possession of the Equipment shall pass to Donee upon delivery of the Equipment.  
Donee shall have no right to possession or occupancy of or entry upon any portion of the property 
where the Equipment is currently located other than as provided in Section 2 above.  Title to the 
Equipment shall remain vested in Donor until Closing. 
 
(d) 
The Closing shall be held virtually at the convenience of the parties. 
 
(e) 
At the Closing, Donor shall deliver to Donee the executed Quitclaim Bill of Sale 
referred to in Section 4 for the units of the Equipment, and Donee shall deliver to Donor an 
executed counterpart of the Quitclaim Bill of Sale and any documents required by Donor as set 
forth in Section 8 below. 
 
(f) 
At Closing, Donee shall pay to Donor any costs required to be reimbursed or paid 
to Donor by Donee under subsection 2(b) above.

Section 4.  Title. 
 
Upon Closing as set forth in Section 3, Donor’s right, title and interest in and to the 
particular units of the Equipment shall be transferred by Donor to Donee by a duly executed

Section 5.  Compliance with Laws.  
  
 
It is understood that the sale and conveyance made pursuant to this Agreement shall be 
subject to any and all applicable federal, state and local laws, orders, rules and regulations. 
 
Section 6.  As Is; Release and Indemnity)

Section 7.  Removal of Union Pacific Marks.   
 
 
Prior to any public display or operation of any units included in the Equipment, except as 
may be agreed by Donor in writing, unless Donee has removed all Union Pacific marks from such 
units, Donee shall install external signage on such units in a form reasonably acceptable to Donor 
that such units are “Not operated by Union Pacific Railroad”.  Additionally, Donor reserves the 
right to require Donee to remove from the each of the units any external marks, logos, livery, 
signage or other markings identifying such units as owned by or related to Donor or any of Donor’s 
affiliates.  Additionally, Donor reserves the right to require Donee to remove from the interior of 
each of the units included in the Equipment, any Union Pacific marks, logos, signage or other 
markings identifying such units as owned by or related to Donor or any of Donor’s affiliates.

Thank you for choosing Vintage Rail Restorations, hereafter VRR, to review your restoration project. 
This is a very exciting project for VRR and we look forward to being able to participate in this 
restoration with you. 
Project  Manager  Mike  Pannell
VRR has assigned Mike to be your project manager. Mike has a passion for antique machines of all 
kinds including automobiles, buses, railroad equipment, and aircraft. Mike has been deeply involved in 
projects involving railroad, aircraft, and bus restorations. Some of these projects are personally 
owned by him including a vintage Colorado & Southern passenger car, early Union Pacific 
Cabooses, and Bristol double-decker buses. Mike made the transition to do what he loves on a full-
time basis by founding Vintage Rail Restorations in 2020. Mike will ensure that your restoration 
project(s) will be completed properly whilst maintaining as much of the history of the item as possible.

Pro
roject C
t Components  
The Cheyenne Railcar Renovation Project includes three main railroad components.  
Component 1 is Union Pacific Steam Locomotive 1242, a 1890s steam locomotive which has been static 
displayed in a Cheyenne park for many years. The project involves lifting, loading, and moving the 
locomotive from Lions Park, Cheyenne to 15th Street in Cheyenne where it will be stripped down, asbestos 
abated, rebuilt and repainted for static display on rails. 
Component 2 is Southern Pacific business/pas- 
senger car 7077. This car will be moved from the 
Cheyenne’s Union Pacific rail yard to 15th Street 
to be displayed with Locomotive 1242. This project 
involves stripping the car down for exterior and 
interior repainting, repairing and replacing any rotten 
wood and steel, and making the roof watertight.
Component 3 is Union Pacific 1959 Caboose 812. 
The project involves moving this car from Cheyenne’s 
Union Pacific rail yard to 15th Street. Restoration will 
include exterior paint stripping, patch welding where 
required, and new interior and exterior paint.  
Project Overview 
Project Overview 
The Cheyenne Railcar Renovation Project entails the renovation of a historic train engine, 
caboose, and passenger car, as well as transportation of the three cars to a downtown 
attraction site. The three cars will be the start of an expanded railroad attraction near 
Cheyenne’s historic train depot along the Union Pacific railroad tracks. The engine will 
remain as a historic artifact for viewing, while the other two cars may be displays or 
specialty retail or dining shops.

Feasibility Summary
Feasibility Summary
Vintage Rail Restoration judges the Cheyenne Railcar Renovation Project to be feasible for successful 
completion. The community has an engaged City administration dedicated to the project, ownership of the 
project components and necessary land, experienced and expert companies in the area that can complete the 
work, and the resources necessary for the work. 
Vintage Rail Restoration anticipates the following needed permits:
1. Asbestos abatement
2. Lead paint 
3. Railroad car transfer (transfer of Components 2 & 3 in the Union Pacific Railyard)
4. City traffic (transfer of Component 1 on city streets)

Vintage Rail Restorations: Proposal & Pricing Agreement
6
Scope of Work
The following items are included in the project estimate.
Locomotive 1242 is currently in Lions Park on display and will need to be loaded and moved to 
15th Street using trucks and cranes. The locomotive is 63ft 6in long and weighs 196,000lbs. Once 
removed from 15th Street, it will be covered with a temporary building, and a new boiler jacket 
fabricated. Rotten wood will be replaced, sandblasted and repainted. The locomotive will then be 
put on display.
Business Car 7077 is 85ft long, and weighs 140,000lbs. It will be moved from the UP rail yard to 
15th Street using cranes. Once removed, it will be covered with a temporary building.
Rotten wood and steel will be replaced, roof made water-tight, paint stripped and repainted and 
put on display.
UP Caboose 812 is around 70,000lbs and is 40ft long. The caboose will be removed from the UP 
rail yard using cranes, where it will be covered using a temporary building. Metal and rotten 
wood will be replaced as needed, paint-stripped and repainted internally and externally, and then 
put on display.

Weather Considerations
Weather is always a factor for outdoor restoration projects. Outside temperatures must be in ideal
ranges to sandblast, prime, and paint projects. When weather conditions are not ideal, VRR may be
forced to halt work, and wait for better conditions to provide the expected quality for the project that
you desire. Delays caused by weather are not normally factored into the cost consideration. Delays
caused by weather may result in higher final project costs as a result of unanticipated lodging, food, 
and travel. Delays caused by weather are out of our control. When weather delays occur, VRR 
diligently makes an effort to work with its customers to keep costs down. Customers of outdoor
projects must be aware of the potential for delays and/or increased costs due to weather conditions. 
Time and Materials Projects
Restoration, construction, and refurbishment projects are considered “Time and Materials Projects” and
are NOT, in any way considered to be Firm Fixed Price contracts. It is impossible to foresee every
situation that may arise in a restoration project. Even if a job walk and detailed scope are provided,
if the project requires demolition, fabrication, and restoration, there are and will be unknown variables.
VRR is not responsible for lack of clarity in the Scope of Work provided by VRR or the customer.
Items that arise that are outside of the Scope of Work – either defined by the customer or by VRR –
shall be considered a Change Order (see below) wherein the customer has the right to approve or deny
the Change Order request being made by VRR. The customer may also request a Change Order and
VRR has the right to accept  the requested Change Order or deny it, which typically is due to the
Change Order requiring skills or abilities outside of our own. VRR reserves the right to stop work until
invoicing issues are resolved. 

Vintage Rail Restorations: Proposal & Pricing Agreement
8
Change  Orders
Attached at the end of this  proposal is a blank “Change Order Form” which may be used by either 
party to the Agreement to make Change Requests to supplement the original Pricing and Proposal 
Agreement. As is noted above in the Time and Materials Projects section of this proposal, both the 
Customer and VRR have the authority and right to initiate a Change Order request. Change Orders 
require the signature of both parties to be valid.  Incomplete Change Order Forms are not considered 
part of the project until they are fully and properly executed by both parties. 
In the event that the Customer or VRR cannot fully execute or complete a Change Order when 
required, the Customer must understand that work on the project may halt until such a time that the 
issue can be fully resolved to the satisfaction of both parties. Some discovered items generating a 
Change Order may be items that, until fully addressed due to their nature, may prevent other work from 
being completed.
Payment
Required Deposit
Projects that contain materials and/or up-front costs shall require a deposit. This deposit will be used
against the projects as it begins and progresses. In the case of this project, please refer to the Proposal
and Pricing Agreement page below.
Invoicing Requirements
Please read carefully, study and review the included Schedule of Billable Services. This project will
require weekly invoicing (unless otherwise agreed to by both parties) with payments due Net 10, ACH
Direct Deposit.

1 
 
SECTION 1.  PARTIES 
 
The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipal 
corporation, hereinafter referred to as the “City,” and the Cheyenne Firefighters, Local Union 
No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, hereinafter referred to as the “Union.” 
 

2 
 
SECTION 2.  DEFINITIONS 
Acceptable Fire-Training Academy: 
A professionally certified school whose program is the equivalent of the academy provided by 
the Front Range Fire Consortium. 
Bargaining Agent: 
Local Union No. 279 is the agent representing the firefighters for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and shall be recognized as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent and shall have the 
scope of authority to bargain for rates of pay, hours of work, and other working conditions. 
Base Pay: 
The agreed rate of pay for a firefighter based on their rank and step as specified in Section 21 
excluding overtime pay, longevity pay, holiday pay, education pay, and specialty pay. 
Cadre/Training Instructor: 
The Cadre/Training Instructor position will be utilized to fulfill Cheyenne Fire and Rescue’s 
obligation to the Front Range Fire Consortium, its need for qualified instructors and to offset 
associated costs with participation.  It may also be used to meet the training needs of the 
Department outside of academy opportunities.  Assignments made to this position shall not 
exceed twelve months in duration.  If a Member is retained for time periods outside of an active 
academy, the Member shall be utilized to meet the training demands of Cheyenne Fire Rescue.  
An assignment made to fill this position shall not violate Section 15, unless otherwise agreed 
upon between the Fire Chief and the Union. 
Civil Service Commission: 
The City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 

3 
 
Command Staff: 
The Fire Chief, Deputy Fire Chief.  
Command Officers: 
Battalion Chiefs 
Cross Staffing:  
The functioning of a comprised company to split its personnel to operate specialized apparatus at 
the same scene. Specialized apparatus shall not be utilized at companies where the call 
concurrency rate exceeds fifteen percent. 
Company Officers: 
Lieutenants. 
Day Personnel: 
A Member who works an eight or ten-hour tour of duty. 
Days: 
For any period of time specified in this Agreement, days shall be computed in accordance with 
the Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Department: 
The Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department. 
Deputy Fire Chief: 
Assistant to the Fire Chief, acts as Fire Chief in their absence and can satisfy the job 
qualifications outlined in the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023. 
Dual Assignment:  
The functioning of a comprised company to split its personnel to operate different apparatus, 

4 
 
separated and on different scenes. 
Eligibility List: 
A list established by the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission pursuant 
to its Rules and Regulations.  
E.M.S.: 
Shall mean Emergency Medical Services. 
Engine Company: 
A pumping apparatus of at least 750gpm capacity, consisting of a minimum three-person crew 
that is comprised of at least a lieutenant, engineer, and firefighter, of which at least one is a 
paramedic.  
E.O.D.: 
Shall mean earned off-duty time or compensatory time off. 
 
Extraordinary Circumstances: 
An event or condition which could not reasonably be anticipated or expected to occur and is 
beyond the usual, customary, or regular duties of firefighters. 
Fire Chief: 
The Chief of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department and can satisfy the job qualifications 
outlined in the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023. 
Grievance: 
The procedure for the resolution of disputes arising under this Agreement as specified in Section 
8. 
Immediate Family: 
 

5 
 
 
Spouse  
 
 
Grandmother 
 
Daughter 
 
 
Grandfather 
 
Son 
 
 
 
Grandchildren 
 
Mother 
 
 
Mother-in-Law 
 
Father  
 
 
Father-in-Law 
 
Sister  
 
 
Sister-in-Law 
 
Brother 
 
 
Brother-in-Law 
 
Step-Mother  
 
Daughter-in-Law 
 
Step-Father 
 
 
Son-in-Law 
 
Step-Children 
 
Incident Commander: 
The highest-ranking Officer who initially responds to any call for service or who has accepted 
transfer of command. 
Ladder Company: 
An aerial apparatus of at least 75’ with pumping capabilities, consisting of a minimum four-
person crew that is comprised of at least a lieutenant, two engineers, and a firefighter, of which at 
least one is a paramedic.  
Members: 
All regularly sworn employees of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, as covered by the 
Collective Labor Agreement (excluding the Fire Chief and Deputy Fire Chief). 
Officer in Charge: 
Any Officer in a Member’s chain of command. 
On-Call: 
Members may be required to be available for callback for the performance of duties as 
determined by the Fire Chief and/or Deputy Fire Chief, and as compensated in accordance with 
Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages.  
Platoon Personnel: 

6 
 
A Member who works a twenty-four-hour tour of duty. 
Rank: 
Any of the ranks specified in the Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department 
Civil Service Commission. 
Rescue Company: 
An initial attack pumping apparatus of at least 250gpm capacity, consisting of a minimum three-
person crew that is comprised of a lieutenant, an engineer, and a firefighter, of which at least one 
is a paramedic.  
Rules and Regulations: 
The Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne Fire Department Civil Service Commission. 
Seniority: 
The time of consecutive service a Member has accumulated from the date of the Member’s 
employment and shall not be interpreted as time in grade or rank. 
Set:  
Two consecutive 24-hour shifts, served on the Member’s assigned shift. 
“Shall” and “May”: 
“Shall” is mandatory; “May” is permissive. 
Shift: 
The number of hours worked in a tour of duty by: 
A. 
Day Personnel - Personnel assigned to work a forty-hour week (Monday through Friday). 
B. 
Platoon Personnel - Personnel assigned to work twenty-four-hour shifts. 
Shift Trainers 

7 
 
Personnel who have been assigned to assist Fire Training or EMS.  
Specialty Apparatus: 
Any fire department apparatus or vehicle that is intended for specific use by duty personnel for 
their response. These are not defined Company apparatus and examples include, but not limited 
to brush/wildland apparatus, boats, UTV’s, hazardous materials response vehicles, and high/low 
angle rescue response vehicles. 
Specialty Team 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue personnel assigned to the Hazardous Materials and Specialized Rescue 
Teams.   
Squad Company: 
A rescue apparatus capable of transporting patients, consisting of a minimum two-person crew 
that is comprised at a minimum of a firefighter EMT-P and a firefighter EMT-B. Assigned 
members shall be at least a Firefighter Step 2. Squads shall not be dispatched to structure fire 
responses as a single resource.  They may be attached/assigned to a Ladder and/or Engine 
Company and will operate under that company officer on the fire ground. 
Temporary Assignment: 
An assignment to a position and rank for a period of no more than twelve months to meet the 
needs of the Department.  The Department shall not assign more than one Member at any given 
time.  
Vacancy: 
A vacancy is any open position resulting from resignation, termination, retirement, death, 
promotion, or voluntary or involuntary demotion.

8 
 
SECTION 3.  EXCLUSIVE BARGAINING AGENT 
 
The paid Members of the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, the regularly constituted 
Fire Department of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, by a majority vote, have selected the 
I.A.F.F. Local Union No. 279 as the sole and exclusive bargaining agent for all of the Members 
of the Department, except the Fire Chief and the Deputy Fire Chief. 
 
The Union and the City’s obligation to negotiate is subject to Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-10-101 
through 27-10-109. 
 
The City shall not enter into any other agreement with Members serving as firefighters, 
individually or collectively, or with any other organization, which in any way conflicts with the 
provisions of this Collective Labor Agreement. 
 

9 
 
SECTION 4.  TERM OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Agreement shall become effective 12:00 a.m., July 1, 2023, and remain in effect 
until 11:59 p.m., June 30, 2025. 

10 
 
SECTION 5.  SEPARABILITY 
 
If any clause, sentence, paragraph, or part of this Agreement, or the application thereof to 
any person or circumstance, shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction 
to be invalid, such judgment shall not affect, impair, or invalidate the remainder of the 
Agreement. 

11 
 
SECTION 6.  EMBODIMENT OF AGREEMENT 
 
This Collective Labor Agreement constitutes the sole and complete agreement between 
the parties and embodies all the terms and conditions governing the wages, rates of pay, and 
working conditions of the Members represented by the Union.   All costs associated with the 
arbitration shall be shared equally by the City and the Union.  The parties acknowledge they 
have had the opportunity to present and discuss any subject germane to collective bargaining.  
Any prior commitment or agreement between the City and the Union or any individual Member 
covered by this Agreement that has been negotiated by the Union is hereby superseded. 
 

12 
 
SECTION 7.  RE-NEGOTIATIONS 
 
At any time prior to the expiration date of this Agreement, any provision herein may be 
re-negotiated by mutual agreement of the City and the Union.  No part of this Agreement shall be 
opened until mutual agreement is reduced in writing and signed by both parties, consisting of the 
bargaining committee for the Union and the Governing Body of the City.  Only the issue(s) in 
question shall be re-negotiated, and not the entire Agreement. 
 

13 
 
SECTION 8.  GRIEVANCE PROCEDURES 
 
A grievance is defined as a dispute or difference of opinion between the City and a 
Member, Members, or the Union over the meaning, interpretation, application, or the inequitable 
application of an expressed provision of the Agreement.  Any Member may file a grievance 
under this Agreement protesting the exercise of Management Rights, but to prevail, the Member 
must establish a violation by the City of the specific terms of this Agreement.  Any grievance 
filed against the City shall be in writing and if a Member’s grievance, it shall contain the name 
and position of the affected Member(s).  The grievance shall contain a clear and concise 
statement of the grievance, the issue or issues involved, the relief sought, the date the incident or 
violation took place, and the specific section or sections alleged to have been violated. 
 
For a grievance to be considered timely, the Union’s Grievance Committee must submit 
it in writing within ten business days of the date the grievant knew of the event giving rise to the 
dispute, following the steps below: 
Step 1 
 
The Union’s Grievance Committee may submit the grievance in writing to the Deputy 
Fire Chief who will then schedule a meeting with the grievant and two members of the Union’s 
Grievance Committee within ten business days to discuss the grievance and attempt to resolve 
the grievance.  A written decision from the Deputy Fire Chief shall be delivered to the grievant, 
Union, and Fire Chief within five business days of the meeting.  If the decision fails to satisfy the 
grievant and/or Union, either may initiate Step 2 of the grievance process. 
Step 2 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree with the Step 1 

14 
 
response and still feels that they have been grieved, may initiate Step 2 and forward the original 
grievance and written responses from Step 1 to the Fire Chief within ten business days of receipt 
of the written decision from Step 1. 
 
Upon receipt, the Fire Chief shall have five business days to schedule and meet with 
grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee to hear the grievance and associated facts to seek 
a resolution.  If a resolution is reached, the decision shall be reduced to writing and signed by all 
parties within ten business days.  If a resolution cannot be reached, the Fire Chief shall deliver a 
decision to the parties involved and Mayor within ten business days.  If the decision fails to 
satisfy the grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee, either may initiate Step 3 of the 
process. 
 
It is understood that nothing shall preclude the Grievance Committee from electing to 
skip Step 1 and begin with presenting a grievance to the Fire Chief if it is deemed that such 
action is warranted by the nature or circumstances of the grievance. 
Step 3 
 
A grievant and/or Union’s Grievance Committee who does not agree to the Step 2 
response and still feels they have been grieved, may initiate Step 3 and forward all original 
grievance and responses to the Mayor within ten business days of receipt of the Fire Chief’s 
decision. The Mayor and Fire Chief shall meet with all parties involved within ten business days 
of receipt of the Step 3 notification. 
 
In attendance at the meeting shall be the Mayor or their designee, the aggrieved party, the 
Union’s Grievance Committee, and at the Union’s option, its legal counsel.  If the grievance is 
settled as a result of such meeting, the settlement shall be reduced to writing and signed by the 

15 
 
parties within five days following the meeting.  If no settlement is reached, the Mayor or their 
designee shall give a written answer to the grievance within five business days following the 
meeting.  The grievance may then be submitted to arbitration by the Union. 
Arbitration 
 
Within five business days from the receipt of the Mayor or their designee’s written 
answer referred to above, the City and the Union shall request in writing that the American 
Arbitration Association shall submit a list of five arbitrators.  Within five business days after 
receipt of said list, one impartial arbitrator shall be selected.  A coin flip shall determine which 
side will be first in striking a name from the list.  The name last remaining shall be the appointed 
impartial arbitrator. 
 
Arbitration shall proceed pursuant to the provisions of the Uniform Arbitration Act.  
(Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-36-101 through 1-36-119).  The selected arbitrator shall have no right to amend, 
modify, alter, add to, or subtract from the provisions of the Agreement.  The arbitrator shall only 
consider and make a decision with respect to the specific issue or issues submitted and shall have 
no authority to make a decision on any other issue not so submitted to the arbitrator.  In the event 
the arbitrator finds a violation of the terms of the Agreement, the arbitrator shall fashion an 
appropriate remedy.  Any dispute, claim, or grievance arising from or relating to interpretation or 
application of this Agreement shall be submitted to arbitration administered by the American 
Arbitration Association under its Labor Arbitration Rules, unless the parties otherwise have 
agreed in this Section.  The parties further agree to accept the arbitrator’s award as final and 
binding on them, subject to the right of either party to seek judicial review pursuant to Wyo. Stat. 
§§ 1-36-101, et seq.  The parties shall each pay half the costs and expenses of the impartial 

16 
 
arbitrator.  Should the same issue be submitted to arbitration two or more times during the term 
of this Agreement and the same party loses two or more times on that issue, the losing party shall 
pay all costs and expenses of the second or any subsequent impartial arbitration. 

17 
 
SECTION 9.  NON-DISCRIMINATION 
 
Neither the City nor the Union shall discriminate against a Member of the Department by 
any means whatsoever because of race, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, creed, color, 
national origin, or Union affiliation with I.A.F.F. Local Union No. 279.  The City and the Union 
shall comply with the requirements of Title VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, as amended, the 
Fair Employment Practices Act of 1965, as amended (Wyo. Stat. §§ 27-9-101, et seq.), and other 
applicable state and federal laws. 
 

18 
 
SECTION 10.  ANNEXATIONS 
 
The City and the Union agree that should the City annex any new areas the Department 
will provide first response to all calls in all annexed areas with the exception of the F. E. Warren 
Air Force Base. 
 

19 
 
SECTION 11.  VOLUNTARY DEMOTION 
 
Any Member who voluntarily requests a demotion from the Member’s current rank shall 
be demoted to the rank held immediately prior to the Member’s promotion.  The Member shall 
make the request in writing at least thirty days prior to the effective date of the requested 
demotion.  The request shall be subject to approval by the Fire Chief.  The Fire Chief may not, 
prior to the expiration of the thirty-day period, appoint another Member to the position from 
which the Member wishes to be demoted.  Upon the effective date, the Member shall return to 
their previous rank and grade. 
 

20 
 
SECTION 12.  UNION REPRESENTATION 
 
In the event any Member of the Department appears before the Deputy Fire Chief, Fire 
Chief, the Mayor or their designee, for work-related disciplinary action, that Member shall have 
the option of having one elected Union representative present.  The person or persons being 
disciplined shall only be called in while on duty.  The elected Union representative shall be 
granted time off without loss of wages. 
 
For the purpose of this Section, the term “work-related disciplinary action” includes, but 
is not limited to, verbal or written reprimands, demotion, suspension, or termination actions that 
become part of the Member’s personnel file. 
 

21 
 
SECTION 13.  MANAGEMENT RIGHTS 
 
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the City shall have the right to direct 
and manage the operations of the Department and its employees, and to maintain the efficiency 
of the Department including: 
 
1. 
The determination of Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department policy, including the 
right to manage the affairs of the Department. 
 
2. 
The right to establish and modify the amount of apparatus in the main or reserve 
fleet. 
 
3. 
The right to direct the members of the Department, including the right to hire, 
promote, discipline, or transfer Members to another shift. 
 
4. 
The collaboration for the safety, health, and property-protection measurers 
provided by the Department for the citizens of Cheyenne. 
 
5. 
The selection and promotion of employees established by the Civil Service 
Commission in its rules and regulations. 
 
6. 
The determination of policy training of employees. 
 
7. 
The establishment, modification, and enforcement of Department rules, 
regulations, and orders. 
 
8. 
The determination of the amount of supervision necessary. 
 
9. 
The transfer of employees from one station to the other. 
 
10. 
The establishment and determination of specialized emergency response teams to 
include roles, responsibilities, and training requirements. 
 
11. 
The right to establish job descriptions for each duty position within the 

22 
 
department as identified in Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages.  
12.  
The right to staff apparatus and equipment based on community risk analysis 
needs and as identified in Section 2, Definitions and Section 15, Department Staffing.  
 
It is agreed that no conduct or action of the City or Union hereunder shall breach the 
provisions of the Agreement, the Civil Service Rules and Regulations in effect on July 1, 2023, 
and the laws of the State of Wyoming governing Firefighters, and the discretionary power 
invested in the City and the Fire Chief shall not be exercised in an arbitrary or capricious 
manner. 

23 
 
SECTION 14.  STRIKES 
 
During the term of this Agreement, there shall be no strike, slowdown, suspension, or 
work stoppage by the Members of the Department, nor shall there be any lock-out or work 
interference by the City. 
 

24 
 
SECTION 15.  DEPARTMENT STAFFING 
 
The Department shall maintain a minimum staffing level of ninety-two firefighters, 
including the Fire Chief and one Deputy Fire Chief, with twenty-seven firefighters assigned to 
each shift. When Department staffing falls below the minimum level of firefighters, it shall be 
brought back to the minimum level as follows: 
 
The Fire Chief shall utilize the current Civil Service Commission eligibility list to begin 
the replacement process for the available openings.  The Fire Chief may elect to: 
1. 
Hire person(s) who have two or more years of documented paid 
professional firefighting experience, with initial training equal to or higher than the Front 
Range Fire Academy to the level of Firefighter 1 and Firefighter 2.  Additionally, the 
potential new hire must submit all continuing education completed by them as 
documented by their current training division for review.  If the potential new hire meets 
or exceeds the base minimum training as compared to Department standards, the Chief 
may elect to hire the person(s) and have them begin an orientation program and upon 
completion report to shift work; or 
2. 
Hire personnel and send them through the Front Range Fire Academy or 
equivalent program for new firefighter training the to acceptable levels of Firefighter 1 
and Firefighter 2 certification. 
Should a vacancy be filled with a lateral hire, this member shall enter the rank of 
Firefighter. The lateral member’s consecutive years of service from their previous agency 
will determine their step grade but shall not exceed Firefighter Step-1. For example, if a 
lateral hire has two consecutive years of service with another fire department and 

25 
 
conforms to Section 15 subsection 1 of this CLA, they will be paid at the Firefighter 
Step-2 rate. The credit for time does not apply to, or affect, an individual’s seniority date 
or service date, and is therefore not recognized for benefits associated with those dates 
(i.e., promotional time requirements, station picks, vacation selection, seniority points for 
examinations, reduction in force, etc.). This member’s first year of employment shall be 
of a probationary status. The Fire Chief shall have discretion to adjust initial pay step 
with a rate maximum of Firefighter Step-1. 
 
To accommodate fire-training academy schedules, the Department may delay hiring new 
Members until seven days prior to the start of spring and fall academies, while still recognizing 
the importance of minimum staffing levels. 
 
A. 
If a Member separates from service within forty-five days or less of the start of a 
fire training academy program, the City is not required to hire a replacement to attend the 
immediate academy but shall have hired the replacement(s) at least seven days prior to the 
subsequent academy. 
 
B. 
The staffing of the Suppression, Community Risk Reduction (CRR), Support and 
Administration Divisions, excluding the Fire Chief and Deputy Fire Chief, shall be at the 
minimum level of ninety at least seven days prior to the start of the spring and fall academies, 
and shall not fall below the minimum staffing level until another Member separates from service. 
 
C. 
Each new firefighter shall satisfactorily complete an acceptable fire academy 
program or orientation held by either the Department or the Front Range Fire Consortium prior 
to entering the ranks of the Department.  Upon completion of the fire academy, the new Member 
shall be reviewed on a regular basis as set forth in a defined probationary period, approved by the 

26 
 
Department.  During the probationary period, a Member may be terminated at any time without 
cause. 
 
When shift staffing is reduced from vacancies (retirement, separation, or transfer to 
days), the use of E.O.D. time may be restricted to ensure proper shift minimum levels. 
 
D. 
The use of E.O.D. compensatory time is restricted for Platoon Personnel when a 
Member is transferred to a forty-hour week duty assignment or reassigned by the Fire Chief. 
Company Strength 
 
For the safety of the Members and the citizens of Cheyenne, and the effective utilization 
of fire equipment, the Department shall employ engine companies, a ladder company, and a 
Battalion Chief, all without dual assignment and as defined for suppression operations, for a 
minimum staffing of twenty firefighters. The Fire Chief may employ additional resources such as 
an engine, ladder, squad, rescue, specialty apparatus, as the Fire Chief determines as necessary. 
 
All firefighting equipment dispatched to all structural fires shall consist of a minimum of 
sixteen members without an aerial ladder truck and seventeen members with an aerial ladder 
truck. Members on the first alarm, and any alarm thereafter shall be answered at the discretion of 
the Incident Commander. The Incident Commander shall have full authority to request additional 
resources, make appropriate assignments and comply with the 2 in-2 out standard.  
 
In a Mutual Aid request for assistance, the City need only respond with the requested 
equipment.  The on-the-scene Officer in Charge of such equipment may, for safety reasons, 
request additional equipment through the Authority Having Jurisdiction Incident Commander 
before involving the crew at the scene. 

27 
 
SECTION 16.  RESERVE MANPOWER 
 
In the event of an emergency that in the judgment of the Incident Commander 
necessitates additional manpower, the Incident Commander may call in off-duty personnel of the 
Department.  The Incident Commander may request mutual aid assistance when justified by the 
circumstances. 
 

28 
 
SECTION 17.  HOURS OF WORK 
 
The average work week for Platoon Personnel shall be fifty-six hours computed on a 
twenty-four-day basis.  The three-shift system shall be maintained with twenty-four-hour tours of 
duty, and the existing schedule shall be continued, which will include scheduled work time from 
the hours of 0800 to 1700 with one hour off for lunch and one hour for physical fitness training. 
 
Beginning January 7, 2023, the employer shall begin the work shifts per work period as 
follows: the stated work period for purposes of complying with the Fair Labor Standards Act 
shall be twenty-four days.  Each Member shall be assigned to work shifts that total 192 hours of 
work per period.  The assigned schedule shall be a 48/96 shift schedule, three platoon system in 
which employees work two consecutive twenty-four hour shifts for a total of forty-eight hours 
and have ninety-six consecutive hours off. The maximum number of consecutive hours worked 
by members shall be ninety-six hours. 
 
 
Day Personnel will be primarily scheduled to work four 10-hour days (40-hour 
work week) as defined in the definitions.  Shifts may begin at 0700 hours and may end at 1800 
hours. This time may be adjusted to meet the needs of the Fire Department or the Member with 
the approval of the Fire Chief.  The schedule may have one weekday, and shall have Saturday, 
Sunday, and holidays off.  If a holiday falls on a Member’s regularly scheduled day off, the 
Member shall receive the day before the holiday or the next regularly scheduled workday off, as 
determined by the Fire Chief. 

29 
 
SECTION 18.  TRADING SHIFTS 
 
Members shall be permitted to trade shifts or parts of a shift if equal duties can be 
assumed with permission of the Battalion Chief or designee, providing there is no additional cost 
to the City.  No Member shall be permitted to compensate another Member for a shift worked, 
either in money, or in any other form of remuneration.  
 

30 
 
SECTION 19.  INCLEMENT WEATHER CONDITIONS 
 
It is the responsibility of each Member of the Department to make an honest effort to 
report to work as scheduled.  During periods of severe inclement weather, Members are 
classified as essential personnel. 
 
If a Member cannot report for duty as scheduled without an unreasonable risk to their 
safety, the Member shall immediately notify an Officer in Charge at Headquarters by the hours 
specified in the rules and regulations to report the Member’s status.  The following will apply to: 
Platoon Personnel: 
 
The Officer in Charge shall notify the Member who has to be relieved of duty and place 
that Member on holdover until properly relieved. 
 
The affected Member shall be: 
1. 
Granted two hours of administrative leave without penalty to report to 
duty.  If a Member cannot arrive safely in the two hours, the Member may request from 
the Fire Chief or Deputy Fire Chief an additional two hours of administrative leave 
without penalty (maximum of four hours administrative leave). 
2. 
If the Member is still unable to safely report to duty by noon, they may 
obtain an emergency time swap for the period needed to report to duty or utilize accrued 
vacation or E.O.D. time in hour increments until they are able to report to duty. 
Day Personnel: 
 
Members who work a forty-hour work week shall be required to observe the following: 
If the City remains open and a Day Personnel Member cannot safely report for 
duty, the affected Member will contact their appropriate supervisor to report the 

31 
 
Member’s status.  The Member affected by inclement weather will be allowed to flex the 
needed time to safely report to duty or utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour 
for hour basis for time absent until they are able to safely report to duty. 
If City offices are closed, or a delayed start is declared by the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, Day Personnel will be granted time off without penalty. 
In the event of a delayed start, Day Personnel will be required to report for duty at 
the designated time or will be allowed to flex the needed time to safely report to duty or 
utilize accrued vacation or E.O.D. time on an hour for hour basis for time absent until 
they are able to safely report to duty. 
The Department will not charge accrued vacation or E.O.D. compensatory time 
for time missed in the event a Day Personnel Member is directed by the Fire Chief or 
their designee to leave work early due to inclement weather. 
 

32 
 
SECTION 20.  HOLIDAYS 
 
The following are the recognized paid holidays for this contract period for platoon 
personnel only. Day personnel shall be off on days that are congruent with the City of Cheyenne 
holiday schedule, with full pay.  
 
New Year’s Day 
 
Martin Luther King, Jr., Day 
 
President’s Day 
 
Memorial Day 
 
Juneteenth National Independence Day 
 
July 4th 
 
One-half day for Cheyenne Day 
 
Labor Day 
 
Columbus Day  
 
Veteran’s Day 
 
Thanksgiving Day 
 
Christmas Day 
 
Department Members shall be granted a choice of either E.O.D. compensatory time off 
for the actual number of hours worked during the holiday or paid compensatory wages at their 
regular base rate for that shift on the holiday hours worked and shall not be deprived of and shall 
receive in addition to their regular base pay for the day. 
 
 
The Department shall refrain from scheduling activities on the holidays 
designated herein. 

33
