39 
 
SECTION 22.  OVERTIME 
Overtime rates shall be paid on the following work: 
 
A. 
Holiday overtime. 
 
B. 
Ordered duty overtime and hold over overtime. 
 
C. 
Overtime work in excess of 182 hours per twenty-four-day work period. 
 
When computing total hours in a work period, Members shall be given hour for hour 
credit for the following paid leave: 
 
A. 
Bereavement leave. 
 
B. 
Leave due to injury on-duty. 
 
C. 
E.O.D. 
 
D. 
Vacations. 
 
E.     
Military 
 
All hours worked up to and including 182 hours in a twenty-four-day work period shall 
be compensated at the base rate for the classification that the Member is assigned to as defined in 
Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly 
scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such 
as pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section. 
 
All hours worked in a twenty-four-day work period in excess of 182 hours will be paid an 
additional one and one-half times the base rate as defined in Section 21, Classification of 
Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such as pay for vacation, 

40 
 
sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the purposes of this 
Section. 
 
Overtime shall be paid at the rate of one and one-half times the individual’s base rate, and 
will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays, or the base 
rate of the position they are filling, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays, whichever is higher.  All other remuneration to the Member such 
as pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section.  All personnel shall be granted their choice of overtime pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time off at the rate of one and one-half hours off for each hour worked. 
 
If overtime work is required to cover a vacancy in any position, such work shall be 
offered to Department Members from a roster which includes all Members.  The shift roster shall 
be maintained by the Union and monitored by the Fire Chief and/or their designees, and such 
work will be offered in as equitable a manner as possible.  Any position filled pursuant to this 
Section shall be paid at the Member’s base rate or the rate for the position they are filling, 
whichever is higher. 
 
Overtime work on a holiday shall be paid at an additional two times the base rate for the 
hours that are on the holiday. 
 
All Daytime positions shall be filled at the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 
Mandatory call out pay shall be two hours minimum.  After the two hours minimum, one 
to thirty minutes shall be compensated for as one-half hour, and thirty-one to sixty minutes shall 
be compensated for as one hour. 
 
The maximum accrual for E.O.D. compensatory time will be 480 hours.  This maximum 

41 
 
accrual applies to any balance of E.O.D. compensatory time including, but not limited to, amount 
earned from committee work, special projects or overtime, sick leave bonus.  Any E.O.D. 
compensatory time over 480 hours must be taken during the calendar year in which it was 
earned.  No Member will be allowed to carry over a balance of E.O.D. compensatory time 
greater than 480 hours on December 31.  The use of E.O.D. compensatory time cannot create an 
overtime situation.  Members hired after July 1, 2019, shall be capped at a maximum of 240 
hours of E.O.D. 
When Day Personnel are working in an overtime capacity on a platoon shift, their base 
rate, education, specialty, EMS, and longevity pays shall be converted to a 56-hour pay rate.  

42 
 
SECTION 23.  WORKING IN A HIGHER JOB RANK 
A. 
In the event a Platoon Member promoted above the rank of Firefighter is absent 
from their normally scheduled shift, a qualified Member will assume those duties for 
the duration of the absence. The assigned Member shall receive all regularly 
scheduled base, education, specialty, and longevity pays that the Member is entitled 
to. Members certified to act shall be paid in accordance with Section 21, “Acting 
Certification Pay”. Daytime Members are excluded from Acting Certification Pay. 
Members are eligible for one Acting Certification Pay regardless of the number of 
certifications the member obtains. At the discretion of the Fire Chief, should a 
member act in a higher job rank for more than three consecutive months, that member 
shall be paid the Step 2 rate of said rank. If that member is not qualified to act beyond 
said rank, they will not qualify for acting certification pay while receiving the Step 2 
rate.  
B. 
When filling an absence through the acting mechanism, preference may be given 
to retain the acting Member at the station in which they have been assigned or chosen. 
Additionally, a Member’s ranking on the “eligibility list” may be taken into account 
to provide for said Member to act more frequently. Final acting placement for daily 
operations will be at the discretion of the corresponding shift’s battalion chief. 
C. 
Except in cases of overtime, the Member(s) assigned to fill said position(s) shall 
be from the same shift. In the event there are no Members from the same shift 
available to act, the absence shall be covered by a qualified Member using the 
overtime mechanism. 

43 
 
D. 
Certification eligibility: 
a. 
Members previously certified or who have completed departmental training and 
testing shall be grandfathered. 
b. 
All members must complete departmental training and testing as approved by the 
Fire Chief. The Fire Chief shall establish a career progression pathway for all 
Members. Formal acting academies for Engineer, Lieutenant, and Battalion Chief 
shall be available to those qualified members at a minimum of every two years. 
c. 
Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Engineer must have completed two 
years of service to the Department. 
d. 
Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed 
four years of service to the Department. 
e. 
Engineers assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed four 
years of service to the Department. 
f. 
Lieutenants assigned to act in the capacity of Battalion Chief must have 
completed eight years of service to the Department.   
g. 
Any Member may choose to remove themselves from the acting pool, said 
Member shall not receive any corresponding acting pay referenced in Section 21. 
If the Member chooses to reinstate their name into the acting pool, their 
qualifications and eligibility will be subject to review and approval of their 
departmental training and testing as approved by the Fire Chief. The frequency and 
circumstances surrounding the Member’s choice to remove or reinstate themselves 
may be considered by the Fire Chief when reviewing and approving the Member’s 
reinstatement.  
E. 
In the event a Member occupying a Day Personnel position is absent due to 
illness, injury, disability, or other cause and it is expected that the Member will 
resume the performance of the duties of the position, the Fire Chief may assign 
another Member to the position as an actor.  If the Fire Chief chooses to assign an 
actor, the Fire Chief shall assign the highest-ranking Member on the eligibility list for 

44 
 
the position, established by the Civil Service Commission in its Rules and 
Regulations, who is willing to serve as an actor.  If the eligibility list for the position 
has become exhausted pursuant to Chapter I, Section 3.s., of the Civil Service 
Commission Rules and Regulations, the position shall be offered to Members whose 
names appear on a roster established to fill the position. 
F. 
All Members on the acting roster for Day Personnel positions shall have four 
years of employment with the Department and the Fire Chief may require possession 
of a Class B driver’s license.  In the absence of an established acting roster, the Fire 
Chief may assign a Member to fill the position by temporary assignment.  Any 
Member shall be placed on or removed from a roster by notifying the Fire Chief in 
writing. 
G. 
Light duty, as defined in Section 27 of this Agreement, will not be considered 
working in a higher job rank. 
H. 
A temporary assignment, if assigned will adhere to Section 2 of this agreement, 
with the following conditions: 
a. If an assigned Member’s work schedule is modified, Section 24 of this agreement 
will be followed to convert pay and benefit hours for the duration of the 
assignment.  
b. A Member shall not be converted to a lower rank or classification in the event of a 
temporary assignment. 
c. The Member will continue to receive all specialty, education, longevity, acting 
and other pays the Member is entitled to for the duration of the assignment. 

45 
 
SECTION 24.  CONVERSION OF BENEFIT HOURS 
 
A. 
Members who move from a Platoon Personnel rank or position to a Day 
Personnel rank or position, and Members who move from a Day Personnel rank or position to a 
Platoon Personnel rank or position, pursuant to promotion, transfer, temporary assignment, or 
Light Duty will be subject to benefit conversion.  The benefit conversion will be calculated under 
the following formulas: 
1. 
Platoon to Day:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Days 
divided by hours worked on Platoon per month.  Conversion factor shall be 0.712 
(173.333/243.333) = 0.712. 
Example:  A Platoon Personnel Member has accrued 200 hours times 0.712 for a 
total of 142.4 hours (200 X 0.712) = 142.4. 
2. 
Day to Platoon:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Platoon 
divided by hours worked on Days.  Conversion factor shall be 1.403 (243.333/173.333) = 
1.403. 
Example:  A Day Personnel Member has accrued 100 hours times 1.403 for a 
total of 140.3 hours (100 X 1.403) = 140.3.   
 
B. 
These calculations shall apply to conversions of vacation and sick hours. 
 
C. 
Members with less than thirty days in assignment shall not be covered. 

46 
 
SECTION 25.  WELLNESS/FITNESS PROGRAM 
 
The City agrees to provide an annual physical for all Members per National Fire 
Protection Association 1582 guidelines.  In addition, specialty team members will receive any 
additional testing as required by the standard. 
 
Furthermore, the City and the Union agree to adopt and promote the International 
Association of Fire Chief’s International Association of Fire Fighters Peer Fitness and Wellness 
Program. 
 
This benefit does not constitute a prevailing right. 
 

47 
 
SECTION 26.  VACATIONS/E.O.D. 
 
A. 
Annual vacations shall be determined for the period from January 1 to December 
31 of each year based on the years of service completed prior to January 1 of each year. 
 
B. 
All vacation time shall be accrued. 
 
C. 
All rosters shall be made up prior to January 1 of each year in which the vacations 
are to be taken. 
 
D. 
All Platoon Personnel shall choose their vacations on their respective shift, 
according to their seniority on the job. 
 
E. 
A separate vacation roster shall be maintained for each shift. 
 
F. 
A maximum of five slots may be available at any time, four vacation or E.O.D. 
slots and one E.O.D. only slot, except for override vacations due to transfers or approvals from 
the Fire Chief. 
 
G. 
Vacations shall be picked by seniority of the Shift.  The Senior Member shall 
select complete sets until they are either unable to select more or elects to stop their pick.  The 
next Senior Member shall then select complete sets until they are either unable to select more or 
elects to stop their pick. The sequence shall continue by seniority until the entire Shift has 
picked.  The sequence shall start over by seniority and continue until all Members have 
exhausted their accrued days and/or non-complete sets (individual shifts) from available dates. 
Requests for changes to vacations or E.O.D. shall be made to the Battalion Chief of the Shift and 
shall be awarded on a first come first serve basis on December 1 at 0800. 
 
H. 
If, after sick call, there are additional openings that do not affect minimum 
staffing, Members may request “Day of Vacation or E.O.D.” as long as the additional slots do 

48 
 
not create overtime.  Openings shall be awarded on a first come first serve basis. 
 
I. 
Day Personnel shall be carried on a separate vacation roster from the Platoon 
Personnel, and they may split vacations. 
 
J. 
During the term of this Agreement, a Member’s vacation or balance thereof shall 
be rescheduled into available vacation slots due to promotion and demotion.  If a Member is 
involuntarily moved, that Member retains their slot at their new assignment.  In the event a 
Member is involuntarily moved, and a slot is vacated by any personnel in the four-member 
group, that slot shall be considered vacated and the maximum of four Members off will take 
effect.  In the event there are no vacation slots available, only then will a Member be allowed to 
carry their vacation beyond the maximum four persons off at any one time. 
 
K. 
All Platoon Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits 
as follows: 
After successful completion of the fire academy, a pro-rated amount of 24-hour shifts at 
.8333 shifts per each full month of continuous service to be taken in the following year; 
After one year of completed service, ten 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After five years of completed service, eleven 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After ten years of completed service, twelve 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After fifteen years of completed service, thirteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 
duty; 
After twenty years of completed service, fourteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 
duty; 
After twenty-five years of completed service, fifteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 

49 
 
duty; 
After thirty years of completed service, sixteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty. 
All platoon vacation accrued shall be scheduled and used each calendar year, with no 
carryover of days allowed, unless a member is unable due to injury or illness. 
 
L. 
All Day Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits as 
follows: 
 
After one year of completed service, 120 hours; 
 
After five years of completed service, 140 hours; 
 
After ten years of completed service, 170 hours; 
 
After fifteen years of completed service, 190 hours; 
 
After twenty years of completed service, 220 hours; 
 
After twenty-five years of completed service, 240 hours; 
 
After thirty years of completed service, 260 hours. 
Day Personnel assigned after July 1, 2023, shall not carry over more than 240 hours of 
vacation from one calendar year to the next.  
 

50 
 
SECTION 27.  SICK LEAVE 
 
All Members of the Department shall earn accumulative sick leave at the rate of one shift 
for each full month completed until a maximum of sixty working shifts of sick leave have been 
accrued.  All sick leave not in excess of sixty shifts, which has been accrued by Members of the 
Department at the date of this Agreement, shall be considered to be vested and shall continue in 
effect.  At the time of separation from the Department, Members with at least four years of 
continuous service shall be paid fifty percent of their accrued sick leave up to a maximum of 240 
hours at their regular base rate computed on 2,080 annual hours not to exceed thirty 8-hour 
shifts.  Sick leave is hereby defined to mean absence from post of duty of a Member because of 
personal illness or injury of the Member.  Sick leave may be used for attendance upon an 
immediate family member due to illness, injury, or accident up to a maximum of three sick leave 
shifts per year. 
 
Sick leave shall not be considered as a privilege which a Member may use at their 
discretion but shall be allowed only in case of necessity and actual sickness or disability of the 
Member or immediate family member. 
 
A. 
Beginning January 1, 2014, all Members who take ninety-six hours or less of sick 
leave for a calendar year shall be paid a cash bonus or E.O.D. compensatory time as set forth 
herein. 
 
B. 
All Members shall be eligible for up to ninety-six hours of pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time when they use less than ninety-six hours of sick leave in a calendar year.  For 
every hour of sick leave used, the pay or E.O.D. compensatory time shall be reduced by one hour 
from the possible ninety-six hours.  Members shall only be able to take up to forty-eight hours of 

51 
 
pay.  All hours earned over forty-eight shall be taken as E.O.D. compensatory time. 
 
C. 
If the Member takes any of the first forty-eight hours as E.O.D. compensatory 
time, up to forty-eight of those hours can be banked for future use.  All of the hours earned above 
forty-eight shall be used on or before December 31 of the year in which they were awarded or 
they will be lost. 
 
D. 
This benefit will be prorated for new Members who have been employed for more 
than six months but less than one year. 
 
E. 
After two consecutive working shifts off-duty from illness or injury, a doctor’s 
certificate of examination shall be required if requested by the Fire Chief covering the period of 
sickness or disability.  In addition to the above, a Member may be required to provide a doctor’s 
certificate of examination in the event sick leave is taken for the shift immediately preceding or 
following a vacation period.  Any examination as ordered shall be paid for by the City.  Upon 
presentation of satisfactory information, the Fire Chief may extend the sick leave or waive the 
doctor’s excuse requirement.  If an extension is refused, an appeal may be made to the Mayor. 
 
F. 
Members may, at any time during the calendar year, donate up to one hundred 
twenty hours of their unused sick leave into the Department Sick Leave Bank or the City Sick 
Leave Bank.  A maximum amount of six hundred 24-hour shifts will be accumulated in the 
Department Sick Leave Bank.  Members will be allowed to use sick leave from the Department 
Sick Leave Bank only when all personal sick leave benefits have been used.  Written application 
for the use of the Department Sick Leave Bank must be made to a Sick Leave Bank committee 
consisting of the Union president or their designee, the Fire Chief, and the Mayor or their 
designee, within ten business days prior to the date the sick leave is needed.  The committee 

52 
 
may, at its discretion, request a doctor’s certificate of examination before granting use of the 
Department Sick Leave Bank.  Denial of a sick leave request is not subject to appeal or 
grievance.  Members will continue to accrue benefits during the usage of sick leave from the 
Department Sick Leave Bank. 
 
G. 
Upon separation of employment with the City, Members may donate the balance 
of uncompensated hours to the City Sick Leave Bank. 
 
H. 
Members who are recuperating from an injury or illness may return to duty to 
perform work (light duty) consistent with their abilities as determined by the Member’s 
physician.  The Member shall, upon request, furnish the City with a physician’s statement 
specifying the type of work the Member may perform.  The statement shall specify medically 
necessary restrictions, if any.  The Fire Chief shall place the Member in a position which 
contributes in a meaningful and identifiable way to the goals and needs of the Department. 
 
I. 
Any Member who is injured in the discharge of their duties shall file such reports 
of accident disability or injury, as shall be required to secure the payment of benefits under the 
Wyoming Worker’s Compensation Act to the injured Member.  While injured, said Member 
shall use their accrued sick leave and once exhausted may use the Department Sick Leave Bank 
until return to duty or pension disability is received.  If the Member receives temporary total 
disability payments under the Worker’s Compensation Act, the Member shall transfer those 
monies to the City. 
 
J. 
Claims for medical or hospital charges, drugs, and other compensable medical 
services shall be filed with the Worker’s Compensation Division.  If a Member becomes ill or 
disabled prior to their regularly scheduled vacation, the Member and Fire Chief, by mutual 

53 
 
agreement, shall re-schedule the vacation for said Member into available vacation slots, if 
possible. 
 
K. 
Sick leave will be charged by the hour, one hour for every hour off.  In the event 
that a Member returns to work after being on disability pension, said Member shall return to the 
rank and position they held previous to the disability without loss of seniority if return to work is 
within eighteen months. 
 
 

54 
 
SECTION 28.  BEREAVEMENT LEAVE 
 
In the case of death within the immediate family of any Member of the Department, such 
Member shall be granted leave with pay. 
 
A Member who is assigned to a Platoon Personnel position may have three shifts of leave 
and a Member assigned to a Day Personnel may have four shifts of leave.  Under extenuating 
circumstances, additional leave may be granted by the Fire Chief. 
 
The Fire Chief may, in the exercise of discretion, grant bereavement leave upon the death 
of a person other than the Member’s immediate family. 
 

55 
 
SECTION 29.  LEAVE OF ABSENCE 
 
Any Member of the Department may be granted a Leave of Absence without pay for a 
period not to exceed three months.  The request for such leave shall be made in writing to the 
Fire Chief not less than ten days before such leave would begin and is subject to the Fire Chief 
and the Mayor’s approval.  The Fire Chief may also place a Member on a leave of absence with 
pay. 
 
No Member shall be granted a leave of absence to work in another job full time or be 
newly employed and receive benefits from another employer. 
 

56 
 
SECTION 30.  MEDICAL, DENTAL, AND LIFE INSURANCE 
 
Except as provided in this Section, the City shall pay the premium cost for Health, 
Dental, and Life Insurance under “The City Employees’ Insurance Contract,” for those Members 
desiring to obtain such coverage.  If the City offers more than one health insurance plan to City 
employees, Members shall be offered the plan providing more extensive benefits.  Each 
participant in the City health insurance program shall contribute at the same rate as other City 
employees.  Dental insurance coverage shall be provided for the Member.  Dental insurance 
coverage for the Member’s family is available at the Member’s expense. 
 
A. 
Members shall be entitled to insure their dependents under the plans offered by 
the City.  An eligible dependent is defined as a spouse and children up to the age of 26, whether 
married or unmarried.  Dependent children aged 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if 
a physician certifies that the child cannot support themselves because of a disability which 
occurred before they reached the age of 26. 
 
B. 
Any Member with dependent coverage may add new dependent children at any 
time.  However, the application for coverage must be made within thirty days of the birth, 
placement, or adoption of the child. 
 
C. 
The Union shall have the right to appoint one Member to the City Employee’s 
Health Insurance Committee. 
 
The City shall offer to all Members the option of participating in a flexible health benefits 
plan which qualifies as a “cafeteria plan” within the meaning of Section 125 of the Internal 
Revenue Service Code. 
 

57 
 
 
Whenever any Member’s yearly base salary entitles said Member to a higher amount of 
Life Insurance, such Life Insurance increase shall be effective July 1 of each year with a 
maximum of $20,000.00. 
 
The City shall offer Members fifty years of age or older who elect to retire the option of 
maintaining the current health insurance policy at the single rate.  The City shall pay a maximum 
of $300.00 per month toward the single coverage.  The policy shall remain in effect until the 
Member attains the age of eligibility for Medicare or becomes eligible for coverage through 
another employer.  Members hired after July 1, 2013, shall not be eligible for this provision. 
 
If a Member becomes permanently disabled, as defined by the State of Wyoming 
Worker’s Compensation Division, in the line of duty, or dies due to injury or illness incurred in 
the line of duty, said Member and/or their eligible dependents shall be offered health insurance at 
the expense of the City.  The City shall pay for health insurance for the disabled Member.  Said 
coverage will remain in effect until the Member and their eligible dependents become eligible for 
coverage through another employer or become eligible for Medicare.  Coverage shall be equal to 
or exceed the health insurance available to other Members.  Subject to the conditions in this 
Section, this coverage shall be offered the remainder of said Member’s life, and to the 
dependents of said Member as long as they meet the qualifications of a dependent. 
 
Eligible dependents are defined as: 
 
A. 
The spouse, as long as he or she does not remarry. 
 
B. 
Children up to the age of 26, whether married or unmarried. 
 
C. 
Dependent children age 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if a 
physician certifies that the child cannot support themselves because of a disability which 

58 
 
occurred before they reached the age of 26. 
 
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability 
of Member, of employment, or of a dependent’s age or disability. 
 

59 
 
SECTION 31.  UNIFORMS 
 
A. 
Uniform items and their compliance will be agreed upon between the Fire Chief 
and the Union so as to provide the best options for the Members, while being fiscally responsible 
and assuring for the safety of the Members. 
 
B. 
The City agrees to fund uniform purchases for all Members in an amount of Five 
Hundred Fifty Dollars ($550.00) per Member per year.  The offered uniform items will include, 
but not be limited to, American National Standard Institute (ANSI) compliant work boots, station 
pants, long and short sleeve dress shirts, polo shirt, a tie, Job Shirt sweatshirts, sweatpants, 
workout shorts, coat/jacket, cotton T-shirts (blue, red and white), belts, ball cap, winter cap and 
formal Class A dress uniform and alterations 
 
C. 
A set selection of items for new recruits will be agreed upon between the Fire 
Chief and the Union, or its designee.  Members are responsible for the reasonable care of their 
uniforms. 
 
D. 
The Department shall purchase each member a Class A dress uniform upon 
completion of their fifth year of service. 
 

60 
 
SECTION 32.  PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT 
 
The City will furnish and maintain for each Member serviceable structural firefighting 
turnout gear and equipment consisting of one pair of boots, one pair of trousers that includes 
liners and suspenders, one coat that includes liner, one helmet that includes liner, one pair of 
gloves and a flash hood, and one pair of extrication gloves, as needed.  The City will provide 
coat lights on the truck. 
The aforementioned turnout gear shall be NFPA 1971 compliant.  A second set of turnout 
gear consisting of trouser and coat, a second pair of structural firefighting gloves, and a second 
protective hood shall be provided by the City to all platoon members in order to reduce a 
Member’s exposure to carcinogens after a fire.  Should the Department absorb new operations, 
the Department and the Union will agree upon proper equipment to be issued to each Member.  
Members are responsible for the reasonable care of their equipment. 
 

61 
 
SECTION 33.  UNION DUES 
 
Upon written authorization by a Member of the Department, the City agrees, at no cost to 
the Union or Member, to deduct the sum certified as Union Dues, and the sum certified as PAC 
fund contribution, if any; and deliver the amount collected from each to the Union treasurer in 
their respective amounts.  If a Member does not have a check coming, or the check is not large 
enough to satisfy the sum certified, no collection shall be made from said Member for that 
month. 
 

62 
 
SECTION 34.  MILEAGE ALLOWANCE 
 
If a Member has arrived at their assigned station, and is reassigned to duty at another 
station, said Member shall receive a mileage allowance consistent with City policy for that 
transfer and any other transfers during that shift.  The mileage between stations shall be 
determined by the Fire Chief. 
 

63 
 
SECTION 35.  RESIDENCY 
 
Members will be required to be a resident and be domiciled within a sixty-mile radius of 
the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Headquarters Station 1, located at 716 West 19th Street, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  As used herein, a resident is defined as a person who is domiciled and physically 
resides within the above-described area with the present intent to remain there during the terms 
established above.  Intent may be established by the Member’s physical presence, which is 
ongoing, not temporary.  A postal box alone is not sufficient to establish residency.  Violation of 
this Section may be grounds for termination.  Residency requirements must be met within three 
months of the Member’s date of hire or six months within the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 

64 
 
SECTION 36.  TIME OFF FOR UNION BUSINESS 
 
A maximum of four Members representing the bargaining agent shall be excused with 
pay to attend grievance hearings pursuant to Section 8, and/or the contract negotiations and labor 
management meetings, provided they are attended during their regular working hours. 
 
 
 

65 
 
SECTION 37.  UNION USE OF CITY FACILITIES 
 
The fire station meeting facilities shall be available for Union meetings.  Attendance at 
such meetings by Members of the Union shall be only during off-duty hours, except for those 
Members who are on-duty at the station where such meeting is held may attend after 5:00 p.m.  
Under no circumstance shall the readiness of the Department be compromised during any 
meeting. 
 

66 
 
SECTION 38.  ALCOHOL AND CONTROLLED SUBSTANCE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the current City of Cheyenne Alcohol and 
Substance Policy. 
 

67 
 
SECTION 39.  FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the provisions of the Family and Medical Leave 
Act. 
 

68 
 
SECTION 40.  PREVAILING RIGHTS 
 
Existing working conditions not specifically covered by this Agreement, but established 
through past practice, shall continue in full force and effect and shall not be altered during the 
life of this Agreement except through the mutual concurrence of the parties, except the City may 
properly and equitably exercise its right to make reasonable and prudent changes in operations 
designed to protect the City from liability or improve the service provided by the Department. 
 

69 
 
SECTION 41.  SAFETY AND HEALTH 
 
The City and the Union shall cooperate fully on all matters concerning the safety and 
health of Members of the Department, and the safety, proper maintenance, and repairs of 
Department equipment and buildings. 
 
To ensure that these matters are taken care of in a timely manner, the Fire Chief and the 
Union shall meet within ten days after receiving written notification of the safety and health 
matter.  The Fire Chief will notify and update the Mayor of the matter.  If a resolution has not 
been agreed upon within ten days, the Union shall meet with the Mayor and the Fire Chief. 
 

70 
 
SECTION 42.  LONGEVITY PAY 
 
Each Member who has completed five years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.2796 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.3925 
 
Each Member who has completed eight years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.3962 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.5562 
 
Each Member who has completed eleven years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.5126 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.7196 
 
Each Member who has completed fourteen years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.6290 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.8830 
 

71 
 
 
Each Member who has completed seventeen years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.7457 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.0468 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.8621 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.2102 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty-five years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$1.0951 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.5373 
 
Longevity raises shall be effective the first day of the month following the Member’s 
anniversary date of employment except for those Members who began their employment on the 
first day of the month. 
 

72 
 
SECTION 43.  LABOR/MANAGEMENT RELATIONS 
 
There shall be a labor/management committee consisting of up to six Union 
representatives and six City representatives.  The committee shall meet at least quarterly to 
discuss all matters of mutual concern.  Either party may request additional meetings in the 
quarter as needed.  The committee shall have the authority to make recommendations to the 
Union and the City.  Any recommendation by the committee may be rejected by the Union or the 
City during negotiations. 
 
The Fire Chief and Union shall meet regularly to discuss departmental matters and 
continue to improve working relationships. 
 

73 
 
 
The 2023-2025 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and the Cheyenne Firefighters Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, is 
signed this ________ day of _____________________, 2023. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
 
Attest: 
 
_________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CHEYENNE FIREFIGHTERS, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
LOCAL UNION NO. 279, INTERNATIONAL 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ASSOCIATION OF FIREFIGHTERS 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Vincent McQueeney, President

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_72568020.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
February 21, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement with MHP to Audit Annual Financial Statements 
 
 
On February 27, 2023, the Governing Body will be referring to the Finance Committee a two-year 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Board of Public 
Utilities, and McGee, Hearne & Paiz (MHP) LLP to perform the annual audit for fiscal years 2023 
and 2024.  
The cost to the City for the fiscal year 2023 financial and compliance audit is $74,675, plus $13,500 
to draft the report, for a total of $88,175.  This is an increase of $4,175, or 5%, over the fiscal year 
2022 audit cost.  
The cost of the fiscal year 2024 financial and compliance audit is $78,500, plus $14,175 to draft 
the report, for a total $92,675.  This is an increase of $4,500, or 5.1%, over fiscal year 2023. 
If more than three major federal programs are required to be audited, an additional fee of $3,500 
will be billed to the City per federal program. Prior to the Covid-19 pandemic assistance funds, 
three programs were the average number audited for the City of Cheyenne.  Due to the significant 
increase in federal funds last fiscal year, five major federal programs were audited. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

 
1 of 9 
City Contract # __________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
to 
 
Audit the Annual Financial Statements of the City of Cheyenne  
And its Board of Public Utilities 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 
2023, by and between the City of Cheyenne (“City”), the City of Cheyenne Board of Public 
Utilities (“Board”), and McGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP (“Firm”). 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City and the Board require professional services to audit their annual 
financial statements; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Firm is qualified to provide such audits and has previously audited the 
City and Board for the fiscal years ending June 30, 2022; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Firm has submitted a letter dated February 9, 2023 proposing to continue 
to provide financial and compliance auditing services to the City and Board for the fiscal years 
ending June 30, 2023 and June 30, 2024, a copy of which letter is attached to this Agreement as 
Exhibit A and incorporated by this reference; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Exhibit A allocates fees for the Firm’s proposed services for the City; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Exhibit A allocates fees for the Firm’s proposed services for the Board; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Firm also agrees, at the City’s option, to draft the City’s annual financial 
and compliance report for an additional fee to be paid by the City; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City and Board desire to employ the Firm subject to the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement;  
 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein 
expressed, it is agreed by and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF SERVICES 
The Firm agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Services attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
COMPENSATION 
a. 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
City and Board agree to pay the Firm the costs set forth in Exhibit A.  The Firm 
12:17 pm, Feb 21 2023

 
2 of 9 
shall bill the City and Board separately for each entities respective share.  Upon 
acceptance and approval of the work by the City or the Board, the City and the 
Board respectively shall make payment therefor within 45 days of receipt of said 
invoice.  
 
b. 
With every periodic invoice, the Firm shall provide the City and the Board, 
respectively, with a written statement of the status of the work with respect to the 
Scope of Services.  The Firm shall maintain records supporting time worked and 
direct expenses and shall make those records available to the City or Board at their 
request during normal business hours any time after contract execution and before 
one year after the date of final payment. 
 
c. 
In the event total fees billed by the Firm for a fiscal year to either the City or the 
Board meet the quoted limits specified in Exhibit A for that fiscal year, and the 
work required of the Firm for that fiscal year has not been completed, the Firm shall 
remain obligated to complete said work in its entirety.  
 
3. 
TERM AND EFFECTIVE DATE 
This Agreement shall commence on the date the last required signature is attached hereto 
and shall continue in full force and effect until the scope of services is completed and any 
documents are submitted to the City or the Board in accordance with Exhibit A, unless this 
Agreement is earlier terminated pursuant to Sections 19 and 20 herein. This Agreement 
may be extended upon agreement of the parties. 
 
4. 
MODIFICATION 
Any changes, modifications, revisions, amendments or extensions to this Agreement which 
are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this Agreement shall be incorporated by written 
instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this Agreement. 
 
5. 
INDEMNIFICATION / HOLD HARMLESS 
The Firm agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City or the Board from and 
against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and 
expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise 
as a result of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Firm’s breach of any term or 
provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the 
Firm, or its employees or subcontractors in the performance of this Agreement.  The Firm 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City or the Board pursuant to 
the terms of this paragraph. 
 
6. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City, the Board, their officials and employees, do not waive their governmental 
immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for the parties to 
pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the Agreement.  
Furthermore, the City and Board specifically retain all immunities and defenses available 

 
3 of 9 
to them as sovereign or governmental entities pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., 
and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity.  
 
7. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with proof of the following insurance 
coverages: 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or 
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily 
injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the entire term of the Agreement 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the 
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services 
under this Agreement.  The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with proof of 
workers’ compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Firm shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City and 
the Board from any and all claims arising from the Firm’s negligence in the performance 
of duties under this Agreement.  The City and the Board prefer that this liability insurance 
coverage be provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. 
The Firm shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the City 
and the Board with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is 
or shall be in effect at the time the Firm begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; and 
b. 
In the event the Firm’s services extend into a future policy period, the Firm shall, 
prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City and the Board with a new 
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect 
during all periods of time that the Firm will provide services under this Agreement; 
and 
c. 
The Firm shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that the Firm has provided services under this Agreement; 
and 

 
4 of 9 
d. 
In the event the Firm or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior to the 
expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this paragraph, 
the Firm shall provide to the City and the Board advance written notification of the 
termination of said coverage and shall provide the City and the Board with an 
endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in 
effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that 
the Firm has provided services under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Firm shall name the City and the Board as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City and the Board with a copy 
of the endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
The Firm shall provide the City and the Board with certificates of insurance 
acknowledging the above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this 
Agreement. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon the Firm to immediately notify in writing the City 
and the Board of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in 
the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance coverage required by this 
Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
If requested by the City or the Board, the Firm shall provide the City or Board, 
respectively, with copies of insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City 
and the Board as an additional insured.  The City’s of Board’s failure to request or review 
such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s or Board’s rights or 
the Firm’s obligation hereunder. 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
9. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
10. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Firm shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment 
Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), 
as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any 
properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, and shall not discriminate against any 

 
5 of 9 
individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, 
pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection with the performance under this 
Agreement. 
 
11. 
ALCOHOL AND DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City and Board 
have established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City and Board employees. All parties under contract with City or Board, 
and their employees and subcontractors, are required to comply with the provisions of the 
City’s and Board’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage 
on City or Board property or other sites occupied by the Firm while performing the duties 
and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Firm to familiarize 
themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and 
subcontractors of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Firm, their 
employees or subcontractors are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be 
terminated. 
 
12. 
INDEPENDENT CONTRACTOR 
The Firm shall function as an independent contractor for the purposes of this Agreement. 
The Firm shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that may be incurred 
by the Firm in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this Agreement shall be 
interpreted as authorizing the Firm or its agents or employees to act as an agent or 
representative of or on behalf of the City or the Board or to incur any obligation of any 
kind on the behalf of the City or the Board. 
 
13. 
CONFIDENTIALTY 
To the extent allowed by law, the City, the Board, and the Firm shall treat as confidential 
and not disclose to others information (including technical information, experience or data) 
regarding either party's plans, programs, plants, processes, products, costs, equipment, 
operations, or customers which come within the knowledge of the parties, without in each 
instance securing the prior written consent of the other party, unless such disclosure is 
required by law or legal process. However, nothing shall prevent either the Firm, the City, 
or the Board from disclosing to others or using in any manner information which either 
party can show (a) has been published or has become part of the public domain other than 
by acts of the parties; (b) has been furnished or made known to the parties by third parties 
without restrictions on its disclosure; or (c) was in either party's possession prior to the 
disclosure thereof by the parties to each other. No party shall be restricted from releasing 
information in response to a subpoena, court order, or legal process, but, in the event such 
disclosure becomes necessary, the disclosing party shall notify the other party of the 
demand for information at least seven (7) days prior to disclosing such information so that 
the other party may take any and all steps provided by law to prevent the release of such 
Information. 
 
14. 
FIRM’S RESPONSIBILITIES 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Firm shall perform in a manner consistent 
with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same profession 

 
6 of 9 
currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the same or similar 
locality. Upon notice to the Firm and by mutual agreement between the parties, the Firm 
will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting such a standard. 
 
15. 
NOTICES 
Any notice, correspondence or billing required to be given by the terms of this Agreement 
shall be delivered by hand, or delivered by mail, postage prepaid, to the addresses of the 
respective parties stated below: 
 
CITY:  
Robin Lockman, CPA, City Treasurer 
 
 
2101 O'Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne. WY 82001 
 
 
BOARD: 
Brad Brooks, Director 
2416 Snyder Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
 
FIRM:  
Stephanie Pickering, CPA, Partner 
314 West 18111 Street 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
16. 
CONFLICT OF INTEREST  
In entering this Agreement, the Firm covenants that it presently has no interest, and shall 
not acquire any interest, direct or indirect, financial or otherwise, which would conflict in 
any manner or degree with performance of the services hereunder. In addition. the Firm 
covenants that in the performance of this contract, no subcontractor or person having such 
an interest shall be employed. The Firm certifies that no one who has or will have any 
financial interest under this contract is an officer or employee of the City or the Board. 
 
17. 
ACCEPTANCE NOT WAIVER 
The City's or the Board's approval of reports, and incidental work or materials furnished 
hereunder shall not in any way relieve the Firm from responsibility for the technical 
accuracy of the work. The City's or the Board's approval or acceptance of, or payment for, 
any services shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the City's or the Board's 
rights under this Agreement or any of its legal rights under statute and common law arising 
out of the performance of this Agreement. 
 
18. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
19. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party declares the other party in default hereof, said party declaring default 
shall notify the defaulting party in writing and such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days to cure said default. In the event that the default remains 

 
7 of 9 
uncorrected, the party not in default may elect to: 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount;  
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance; and 
c. 
Avail itself of any other remedy at law or equity. 
 
In the event the Firm fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City or the Board may elect to make good such deficiencies and charge the Firm 
therefore. 
 
20. 
TERMINATION 
The City or the Board may, by written notice to the Firm, terminate this Agreement in 
whole or in part by giving the Firm fifteen (15) days written notice. Upon receipt of such 
notice, the Firm shall: 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected (unless the notice directs otherwise); and 
b. 
Deliver to the City or the Board respectively within five (5) days all data, 
documents, reports, estimates, or summaries, accumulated by the Firm in 
performing this Agreement, whether completed or in process. In event of 
termination, the City or the Board will pay the Firm for accepted work done 
as of the date of termination. 
 
21. 
WAIVER 
The waiver by either party of any term, condition or covenant, or breach of any term, 
condition or covenant, shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, condition or 
covenant, or breach thereof. 
 
22. 
SEVERABILITY  
If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase of this Agreement is 
invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect the validity 
of the remainder of this Agreement, which shall continue in full force and affect. 
 
23. 
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS 
All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure to the benefit of and be binding 
upon the parties hereto and their respective successors and assigns. 
 
24. 
ASSIGNMENT 
No party shall assign this Agreement without prior written consent of both parties. Any 
delegation or assignment shall not operate to relieve either party of its responsibilities 
hereunder. However, nothing in this Agreement shall prohibit the Firm from subcontracting 
to a qualified subcontractor. 
 
25. 
THIRD PARTY RIGHTS 
The parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status of third-party 
beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed so as to create such status. The 
rights, duties and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between the 
parties to this Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this 

 
8 of 9 
Agreement. The parties to this Agreement intend and expressly agree that only the parties 
signatory to this Agreement shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this 
Agreement, to seek any remedy arising out of a party's performance or failure to perform 
any term or condition of this Agreement, or to bring action for breach of this Agreement. 
This paragraph is not intended nor shall it be construed to waive any of the parties' 
immunities. 
 
26. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement, to include Exhibit A, contains the entire understanding of the parties. 
There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this 
matter.

February 9, 2023 
 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, Wyoming  82001 
Attention: Robin Lockman, CPA, Treasurer 
Re: 
Contract Extension Proposal 
 
to provide Financial and Compliance Auditing Services 
 
for the Fiscal Years Ended June 30, 2023 and 2024 
McGee, Hearne & Paiz, LLP (“MHP”) is pleased to present this contract extension proposal to 
continue to provide financial and compliance auditing services to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(the “City”) and the Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities (the “Board”) for the fiscal years ending June 
30, 2023 and 2024.  We value our relationship with the City and the Board and appreciate this 
opportunity to extend our contract.  We believe our past and present involvement with the City and 
the Board, as well as our involvement with numerous governmental entities within Wyoming, 
makes us well qualified to continue serving the City and the Board. 
MHP will conduct these financial and compliance auditing services in accordance with generally 
accepted auditing standards, the standards set forth for financial audits in the current U.S. 
Government Accountability Office’s (GAO) Government Auditing Standards, types of compliance 
requirements described by the Single Audit Act and Subpart F of Title 2 U.S. Code of Federal 
Regulations Part 200, Uniform Administrative Requirements, Cost Principles, and Audit Requirements 
for Federal Awards, and provisions of the Federal Single Audit Act of 1984 (as amended) and U.S. 
Office of Management and Budget (OMB) Circular A-133, Audits of States, Local Governments, and 
Non-Profit Organizations, as applicable.   
MHP is enthusiastic about continuing to serve the City and the Board.  We trust you have found, 
and will continue to find, our experience and capabilities highly responsive to the needs of the City 
and the Board and we intend to demonstrate that MHP continues to be the right choice of an 
accounting firm for the City and the Board. 
 The City and the Board are important to MHP. 
 MHP is independent with respect to the City and the Board. 
 We have a vested interest in the City and the Board, in Laramie County, and in Wyoming.  This 
is where we live and work.  We keep current on the issues facing our community because they 
affect our lives. 
 
EXHIBIT A

 
CONTRACT EXTENSION PROPOSAL 
 
PAGE 2 
 Your audit fees will stay in the community.  The City’s and Board’s Client Service Team is 
chosen from MHP’s highly qualified, well-trained professionals located in our Cheyenne 
office.  Our standards for hiring and promoting are among the most thorough in the 
profession.  Therefore, the City and the Board can be assured that only the most qualified 
professionals are assigned to the audit engagement. 
 You will continue to receive very close attention from your Client Service Team.  The 
personnel assigned to the team, including partners and managers, will be accessible to you 
and proactively looking after your needs. 
 We recognize the value of timely service and will schedule our work according to your plan. 
 We realize the investment the City and the Board have made in MHP over time to help us 
better understand the City and the Board, its people, and its goals.  Like your investment in us, 
we invest our people in you. 
 We structure our services as efficiently as possible to provide you with timely, competent 
solutions at a fair price. 
 We believe that MHP is uniquely qualified to audit the activities of the City and the Board.  
Our people have extensive experience performing financial and compliance audits 
administered by state and local governmental units.  One of MHP’s largest specialized 
industries is governmental units. 
 In size, our governmental clients range from large departments, agencies, and institutions of 
state governments to relatively small units of local government, counties, cities, school 
districts and institutions of higher education.  Nearly all types of governmental activities are 
included.  Because of our strong commitment to the audit of governmental units, and because 
of strong Firm commitments to a high degree of quality, we have developed certain 
specialized audit programs and other material for use in performing these audits.  These 
include audit programs designed for specific types of expenditures and revenue, as well as 
audit software used in sample selection and other audit testing. 
 MHP has the knowledge and experience in governmental audits and accounting systems to 
continue to provide the highest quality auditing in an efficient and timely manner.  Members 
of the Client Service Team chosen for the City and the Board have provided audit and other 
services to the City and the Board and numerous similar governmental entities.  This means 
your Client Service Team will have the expertise and resources to continue to provide 
excellent service to the City and the Board. 
 MHP has developed an integrated approach to financial auditing that significantly improves 
audit efficiency.  Through overall engagement control and personal attention, we will deliver 
the most efficient service possible and will keep your costs commensurate with the benefits 
received. 
 All of our attestation engagements are subject to an engagement administration review, a 
manager review, a partner review, and a report review to ensure that the work is adequately 
performed, reviewed, and approved to confirm that the results obtained support the reports 
issued. 
 
 

 
CONTRACT EXTENSION PROPOSAL  
 
PAGE 3 
 MHP brings the expertise and resources of a national firm to the City and the Board through 
our membership in the RSM US Alliance.  We provide the depth and breadth of resources 
typically available through national firms and the service-oriented perspective you expect 
from your local advisors. 
FEE PROPOSAL 
We recognize that engaging an audit firm is an important investment for the City and the Board.  
Our goal is to provide timely, quality, and responsive services at a reasonable cost that are 
competitive with the current market.  Our fees, like those of other professional service firms, are 
based on actual time spent by our people and are measured by standard hourly rates, reflecting the 
experience level of the professionals assigned to your account. 
In estimating our fees, we anticipate receiving the assistance from the appropriate personnel that 
will allow us to complete our work efficiently.  We will meet with you to discuss the assistance to be 
provided and the timing of when the assistance is to be provided.  We anticipate that the 
appropriate personnel will provide supporting documentation and applicable reconciliations for all 
sample selections.  We will make every effort to minimize time requirements by maximizing the 
effective use of our personnel and utilizing your personnel whenever possible in order to render the 
highest quality service at the lowest possible cost. 
The fee also assumes three major programs audited in conjunction with Uniform Guidance.  Should 
more than three programs need audited, an additional fee of $3,500 per program will be billed.  
Prior to the Covid-19 pandemic assistance funds, three programs were the average number of 
programs audited for the City of Cheyenne. 
Our fees for special requests not directly related to the audit engagement will be based on 
estimated hours required to complete the special request times our standard hourly rates based on 
the level assigned to complete such special requests.  As the scope of those special requests is 
unknown, the hours and rates based on level assigned are our best estimate of pricing for the City’s 
and the Board’s consideration. 
If there are any significant difficulties encountered or accounting, auditing, or regulatory changes 
that impact the scope of these services, we will discuss with you any proposed fee increase and 
obtain your approval before we proceed.  Interim billings may be submitted as work progresses and 
as expenses are incurred.

professional services you deserve.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP 
Subject: Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne; Granite Peak 
Development, LP; Clear Creek Land Company, LLC; Laramie Land Holdings, LLC; Cheyenne 
Logistics Hub, LLC; and Swan Ranch, LLC. 
Date: 
February 27, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne; Granite 
Peak Development, LP; Clear Creek Land Company, LLC; Laramie Land Holdings, LLC; Cheyenne Logistics 
Hub, LLC; and Swan Ranch, LLC.     
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
2/27/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
3/7/2023 
• 
Final consideration 
 
3/13/2023 
 
The purpose of this agreement is to settle past due fees associated with development in the Swan Ranch 
Business Park located generally located in southwest Cheyenne. In conjunction with development of the 
business park a development agreement was executed on May 27, 2011, which was restated on 
September 3, 2013. The restated agreement specified payment of a $1,000 per acre “Public 
Infrastructure Fee” (subject to inflationary adjustments based on the Engineering News Record’s 
Construction Cost Historical Indexes).  The present rate is $1397.38 per acre. The purpose of the 
infrastructure fee is to fund infrastructure enhancements to support the business park.   
 
This agreement intends to memorialize the payment of past due fees by the parties and serve as a 
restatement of the Development Agreements to allow transfer of lands to subsidiaries of Granite Peak 
Development, LP, Clear Creek Company, LLC, and Swan Ranch, LLC, without requiring payment of 
infrastructure fees. The past due fees are the result of extraordinary circumstances and many of the 
more recent fees have been paid. 
 
Staff has determined, and the developer has agreed, that a total of $145,222.02 is due. This total 
amount is based on the fees due at the time of land transfer and excludes transfers to subsidiaries of the 
parent companies, Granite Peak Development, LP; Clear Creek Land Company, LLC; and Swan Ranch, 
LLC. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
The breakdown of payments is as follows: 
• 
Granite Peak Development, LP: $139,308.85  
• 
Clear Creek Land Company, LLC $5,913.15 
 
All fees will be paid within five days of execution of the agreement and will settle past due fees.  
Transfers to other entities will require payment of the infrastructure fee at the current rate at the time 
of transfer.   
 
Attachments: 
1. Memorandum of Understanding 

Page 1 of 2 
AGREEMENT 
The City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of 
Wyoming; Granite Peak Development, LP; Clear Creek Land Company, LLC; Laramie Land 
Holdings, LLC; Cheyenne Logistics Hub, LLC; and Swan Ranch LLC (collectively the “Parties”) 
agree to and stipulate as follows: 
1.
The City of Cheyenne, Granite Peak Development, LP, Clear Creek Land
Company, LLC, and Swan Ranch LLC were signatories to a Development Agreement, which was 
recorded on May 27, 2011, in Book 2221, Page 1156, and an Amended and Restated Development 
Agreement, which was recorded September 3, 2013, in Book 2356, Page 707. (collectively 
“Development Agreements”).  The Laramie Land Holdings, LLC and Cheyenne Logistics Hub, 
LLC are affiliates of Granite Peak Development, LP, Clear Creek Company, LLC, and Swan 
Ranch LLC. 
2.
The Development Agreements required payments of “Public Infrastructure Fees”
at the time properties subject to the Development Agreements that were sold to outside third-party 
entities for development and occupancy of the property.  These fees were set at One Thousand 
Dollar ($1,000.00) per acre and set to increase with an inflationary adjustment.  See pp. 6-7 of the 
Amended and Restated Development Agreement recorded on September 3, 2013.  
3.
The Parties have met and conferred and desire to resolve the payment of all
outstanding Public Infrastructure Fees pursuant to the Development Agreements to date. 
4.
The Parties stipulate and agree that the outstanding balance of all Public
Infrastructure Fees currently due and owing pursuant to the Development Agreements as of the 
date of this Agreement is one hundred forty-four thousand two hundred twenty-two dollars and 
one cent ($145,222.01).  The calculation of all applicable fees, parcels, and acreage are as shown 
on Attachment A. 
5.
Granite Peak Development, LP, hereby agrees to pay the City of Cheyenne the sum
of one hundred thirty-nine thousand three hundred eight dollars and eighty-five cents 
($139,308.85) within five (5) days of the full execution of this agreement by all parties.  See 
Attachment A.  
6.
Clear Creek Land Company, LLC, hereby agrees to pay the City of Cheyenne the
sum of Five Thousand Nine Hundred Thirteen Dollars and Fifteen Cents ($5,913.15) within five 
(5) days of the full execution of this Agreement by all parties.  See Attachment A.
7.
The parties hereby stipulate and agree that the above detailed payments by Granite
Peak Development, LP and the Clear Creek Land Company, LLC resolve all outstanding Public 
Infrastructure Fees due and owing to the City of Cheyenne under the Development Agreements by 
Granite Peak Development, LP, Clear Creek Land Company, LLC, Swan Ranch LLC, Laramie 
11:19 am, Feb 22 2023

Page 2 of 2 
Land Holdings, LLC, and Cheyenne Logistics Hub, LLC, as of the date of the full execution of 
this Agreement by all parties. 
8. 
The City of Cheyenne hereby agrees that the payment of these sums constitutes a 
full and final settlement of all funds due and owing to the City of Cheyenne under the Development 
Agreements for Public Infrastructure Fees with the understanding that any future transfer of land 
to Laramie Land Holdings, LLC, or Cheyenne Logistics Hub, LLC (not in existence at the time of 
the original agreement) shall not count as transfers to a third party and any transfer of land from 
Laramie Land Holdings, LLC, or Cheyenne Logistics Hub, LLC (not in existence at the time of 
the original agreement) to any unrelated third-party agency shall trigger the payment of the fees at 
the time of that land transfer; also noting, however, that Lot 1, Block 1, CFD Park-N-Ride and 
Swan Ranch Rail Park, 2nd Filing (replatted into 10th Filing) will be exempt from Public 
Infrastructure Fees.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 8, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-12-23 for Lift-Equipped Transit Vehicles, for the City of 
Cheyenne, Transit Division 
 ______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-12-23 was solicited for Lift-Equipped Transit Vehicles, for the City of Cheyenne, Transit 
Division. 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Davey Coach. Sedalia, CO.  
2. Creative Bus Sales.  Colorado Springs, CO.  
 
The bids were reviewed by Renae Jording (Transit), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), and Bryan Bell 
(Fleet Maintenance), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Davey 
Coach. 
The bid includes pricing for three (3) calendar years of vehicle orders (2023, 2024 & 2025).  This 
Consideration of Bid is presented for consideration of all three (3) years of pricing, at each year’s 
respective Cost/Each.  Depending on annual funding availability the quantity of vehicles ordered 
may increase or decrease.  This bid was solicited for a minimum of three (3) to a maximum of ten 
(10) vehicles. 
 
The Bidding Documents contain a Surcharge and Escalator – Special Provision, that allows for the 
review of surcharges related to the following items:  Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) 
and/or Body surcharges applied to the cost of the vehicles after the bid opening date & Model year 
escalators.  The Surcharge & Escalator Contingency, is for an amount not-to-exceed Fifteen 
Percent (15%), of the total value of  requested items on the Bid (Specifically for the Year One (1) 
– 2023 Pricing). 
 
The requested Bid award amount is $1,230,573.20 (10 Vehicles Ordered Over 3 Years) + 
Surcharge Contingency $52,000.79, for a grand total of $1,282,573.99.

Bid #S-9-23 was solicited for the Sanitation Division Transfer Station Welding Repairs project.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_54906852.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 21, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Request to Purchase Two (2) Used SUV’s, for the City of Cheyenne, Compliance 
Department 
______________________________________________ 
BACKGROUND INFORMATION ABOUT PURPOSE OF PROPOSED PURCHASE: 
The purpose for this Request to Purchase is to expedite the acquisition of two needed vehicles for 
the Compliance Department.  One vehicle will be used for a new Compliance Building Inspector 
position, and one vehicle will be used for an existing Compliance Building Inspector position.  The 
vehicle for the current position has been experiencing multiple mechanical failures, and has been 
out of service numerous times over the recent past.  
 
PROCUREMENT INFORMATION & PROCESS: 
The City of Cheyenne, Compliance Department, is requesting spending authority in an amount 
not-to-exceed $70,000.00 for the purchase of  two (2) used Mid Size SUV’s.  Each vehicle is 
budgeted at $35,000.00 each. Due to the current market for used vehicles and equipment, timing 
is of the essence when a used vehicle becomes available on the used vehicle market.  Additionally, 
the lead times for new vehicles are growing longer and longer – often exceeding 1 year.   
In accordance with the City of Cheyenne Purchasing Policy, “Selection will be made only from 
vehicles or equipment meeting or exceeding the minimum requirements set forth by the 
department/division. If the amount exceeds $35,000.00 the expenditure must be approved by the 
Governing Body.” 
This is a preemptive request for approval by the Governing Body, to grant spending authority when 
a used vehicle that meets all of the minimum specifications is located & identified.  In order to 
ensure that the City can make an offer on a vehicle in a timely manner, without placing a 
Consideration of Award on City Council Agenda (3-4 Week Process), the Compliance Department 
is requesting this spending authority in advance of beginning the search for a vehicle that meets 
the needs of the Department.  This will provide the City the ability to expeditiously secure used 
vehicles when suitable units are located.  In coordination with the City of Cheyenne Fleet 
Maintenance Division, minimum specifications have been established for this purchase and they 
are attached to this memorandum. 
This Request to Purchase is being concurrently advertised in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle, in 
accordance with the intent of Wyoming Statute § 15-1-113. 
 
Funding:   
020-26-2610-42-43101 (One Cent Sales Tax 19-22 / Capital Equipment Fund) 

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $35,000.00 
Request to Purchase Two (2) Used SUV’s.  The City of Cheyenne, Compliance Department, is 
requesting spending authority in an amount not-to-exceed $70,000.00 for the purchase of two (2) 
used Mid Size SUV’s.  This is a preemptive request for approval by the Governing Body, to grant 
spending authority when used vehicles that meet all of the minimum specifications are located & 
identified. 
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on March 6, 2023 and voted on at 
the March 13, 2022 City Council meeting. 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(307) 773-1045 
 
Published in:  Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
Dates: February 24 & March 3, 2023 



MINIMUM SPECIFICATIONS FOR 
TWO (2) USED MID-SIZE (SUVs) 
 
GENERAL DESCRIPTION: 
Y  N  1. 
Two (2) Used Mid-Size SUVs.  All equipment ordinarily listed as standard 
equipment shall not be removed, unless being replaced by other equipment to meet 
specifications. 
Y  N  2. 
Manufacturer of vehicle shall have a factory authorized service / repair center 
within Laramie, County. 
 
SPECIFICATIONS: 
Y  N  1. 
Model Year 2018 or Newer 
Y  N  2. 
Mileage not to exceed: 50,000 
Y  N  3. 
Four (4) doors with manual or power liftgate and needs to unlock with key fob. 
Y  N  4.

Y  N  5. 
4 or 6 Cylinder Engine. 
Y  N  6. 
Automatic Transmission, 6 speed, Column or console shifter. 
Y  N  7.

Y  N  8. 
Two sets of keys with key fobs and keyless entry and rear hatch/liftgate opening 
with button on fob. 
Y  N  9. 
Exterior main color preference is white, will accept other colors. 
Y  N  10. 
Tires to be All Season, with spare.

Dual front bucket seats with center console and cup holder.   
Y  N  12. 
Front and rear seats to be cloth or vinyl.
