# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-01_TotalExtraction_42143949.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 16, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Committee Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Ave, and via electronic conference meeting, 
for the purpose of collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Firefighters, 
International Association of Firefighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:34 p.m. during 
executive session), Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, 
Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk; Stefanie Boster, City 
Attorney; Jo Jordan, Legal Assistant, City Attorney’s Office; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, and 
Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Mayor Collins advised the meeting would be audio recorded and available on Facebook 
live and stated that Wyoming State Statute §16-4-405(a)(x) of the Public Meetings Act allows the 
governing body to meet in executive session to consider or tender offers regarding salaries, wages 
or other terms and conditions of employment during negotiations with the firefighters union and 
that any motion approved by the governing body to move into executive session during the special 
meetings would be pursuant to this section of statute.    
 
Mayor Collins provided opening comments noting he has been meeting with the Fire 
Department for labor management discussions which have resulted in a better understanding of 
the functions of their department.  Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to Chief John Kopper for 
the work he does with the Union and his representation of the Fire Department and to members of 
the Union’s negotiation team.  The proposed Ground Rules were agreed upon and signed by the 
City and Union. [Clerk’s note: City’s Spokespersons are: Mayor Patrick Collins, City Attorney 
Stefanie Boster and City Council President Richard Johnson.  Union’s Spokespersons are: Neil 
Durante, Vincent McQueeney and Kelsey Raile.]  
 
Following signing of the Ground Rules by Mayor Collins and Union Spokesperson Vincent 
McQueeney both parties presented their respective proposed Collective Bargaining Agreement 
proposals.  Mayor Collins briefly reviewed the City’s proposal which included changes to the 
following areas and Sections: update cover page; update table of contents; Section 2-Definitions; 
Section 3-Exclusive Bargaining Agent; Section 4-Term of Agreement; Section 8-Grievance 
Procedures; Section 12-Union Representation; Section 13-Management Rights; Section 15-
Department Staffing; Section 19-Inclement Weather; Section 21-Classification of Employment 
and Basic Wages; Section 22-Overtime; Section 23-Working in a Higher Job Rank; Section 24-
Conversion of Benefits; Section 26-Vacation/EOD; Section 31-Uniforms; Section 32-Personal 
Protective Equipment; and Section 42-Longevity.  Mr. McQueeney briefly reviewed the Union’s 
proposal which included changes to the following areas and Sections: update cover page; update 
table of contents; Section 2-Definitions, Section 15-Department Staffing; Section 20-Holidays; 

   
Section 21-Classification of Employment and Basic Wages; Section 22-Overtime; Section 23-
Working in a Higher Job Rank; and Section 26-Vacation/EOD  
 
Mr. Johnson moved to meet in executive session, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Those present in executive session 
were:  All members of the governing body; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; Kristina F. Jones, City 
Clerk; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer; Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue; and Jo 
Jordan, City Attorney Legal Assistant.  [Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 6:27 p.m.] 
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:02 p.m.  Union Spokesperson Mr. McQueeney echoed the importance of the labor 
management discussions that occur throughout the year leading up to negotiations.  Mr. 
McQueeney announced the Union’s acceptance of the City’s proposed agreement as written.  
Mayor Collins announced the City’s acceptance of the Union’s proposed agreement as written.  
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:05 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk  
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 25, 2023  
 
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_81771165.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 21, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 12:00 p.m. at 2101 O’Neil Ave. Room 104 and via electronic conference meeting, for the 
purpose of collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional Fire Fighters, International 
Association of Fire Fighters, Local No. 279.  Present were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson (via Zoom), Dr. 
Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, and Jeff White.  Absent: Pete Laybourn and Tom Segrave.  Also 
present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
  
Mayor Collins advised the special session was for the purpose of re-negotiating the existing 
contract labor agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Fire Union to address issues 
involving the Fair Labor Standards Act.  Chief John Kopper, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue Department, 
provided details relating to the Ground Rules for the special session and advised the only Sections 
being addressed were 4, 17, 22 and 26.   The proposed Ground Rules were agreed upon and signed 
by the City and Union.  Vincent McQueeney, spokesperson for the Cheyenne Professional Fire 
Fighters, International Association of Fire Fighters, Local No. 279, provided an offer to the city 
which rectifies issues relating to the Fair Labor Standards Act.  The city announced acceptance of 
the Union’s offer.  Mayor Collins advised the revised contract would come before the governing 
body in November 2022 and would become effective January 7, 2023 if approved.    
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 12:12 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk  
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
October 27, 2022  
 
 
 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_52994216.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 9.04.070, INTERFERENCE 
WITH EMERGENCY CALLS; INTERFERENCE WITH EMERGENCY 
REPORTING SYSTEM, OF CHAPTER 9.04, OFFENSES BY OR AGAINST 
PUBLIC OFFICERS AND GOVERNMENT, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE 
AND WELFARE, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 9.04.070, Interference with Emergency Calls; Interference with 
Emergency Reporting System, of Chapter 9.04, Offenses by or Against Public Officers and 
Government, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Cheyenne City Code is hereby created 
to read as follows: 
 
9.04.070 - Interference with emergency calls; interference with emergency reporting system 
 
A. A person commits a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment for not more than six (6) 
months, a fine of not more than seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00), or both, if he or she 
knowingly obstructs, prevents, hinders or otherwise interferes with the making or 
completion of a telephone call to a 911 system or other telephone or radio communication 
by another person to any emergency response personnel to request protection or other 
assistance from the emergency response personnel or to report the commission of a crime. 
 
B. A person commits a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment for not more than six (6) 
months, a fine of not more than seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00), or both, if he or she 
knowingly calls a 911 system for a purpose other than to report a situation that he or she 
reasonably believes requires prompt service in order to preserve or protect human life, 
health or property. 
 
C. For purposes of this section "911 system" means a telephone system consisting of network, 
database, services and equipment, using the single three-digit number 911 for reporting 
police, fire, medical or other emergency situations and enabling the users of a public 
telephone system, other technology or wireless telecommunications system to reach a 
public safety answering point to report emergencies by dialing 911. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication. 
 
By John Brodie at 11:04 am, Oct 11, 2022

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_28087570.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 2.08.010, APPOINTMENT AND 
TERM—REMOVAL—VACANCY, OF CHAPTER 2.08, CITY OFFICERS 
AND EMPLOYEES GENERALLY, OF TITLE 2, ADMINISTRATION AND 
PERSONNEL, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO ELIMINATE AN OBSOLETE POSITION 
AND UPDATE A PROVISION GOVERNING APPOINTMENT OF 
JUDGES." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 2.08.010, Appointment and term—Removal—Vacancy, of Chapter 2.08, 
City Officers and Employees Generally, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this ordinance. 
 
2.08.010 - Appointment and term; —Removal; —Vacancy. 
 
 
A. At the first regular meeting of the governing body in the official year following the city 
election, or at such other times as needed to fill an appointed position set forth in this 
section, there shall be appointed by the mayor, by and with the consent of the city council, 
the following officers: city clerk, city treasurer, city engineer, city attorney, fire chief, 
police chief, judge of the municipal court,  judge of the juvenile court, public works 
director, planning and development director, support services director, community 
recreation and events director, any and all judges of the municipal court and juvenile 
court, and such other officers and employees as are necessary for the efficient operation 
of the city. 
 
B. Each officer appointed pursuant to subsection 2.28.010(A), shall hold office for a term 
of two years, provided however, that the specific beginning and ending dates of the term 
of appointment shall be specified in the certificate of appointment. 
 
C. The mayor may remove any appointed officer for incompetency or neglect of duty. 
 
D. If a vacancy occurs in any appointed office for any reason, the new appointment shall be 
only for a period to complete the unexpired term of that officer. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, 
clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein (along with some adjacent land not under their 
ownership) as shown in Exhibit A and to make it a part of the City of Cheyenne; 
and 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Adam E. Deschler, a Professional Land 
Surveyor, licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 12045; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e)
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned CB 
Community 
Business 
and 
A-2 
Agricultural 
by 
the Laramie County 
Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Section 4.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
Section 5.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.
